# Pushbutton Ideas

Running log of pushbutton concepts for the AnonGee toolkit, one entry per idea, newest on top.

---

## 2026-09-21 15:41 UTC

**Idea name:** Rebar Joint Clash & Congestion Checker — bar-to-bar proximity and physical-overlap detection between different elements' reinforcement cages meeting at a column/beam/footing joint, the one rebar QA question neither of the repo's two existing rebar auditors asks

**Potential:**
The toolkit checks rebar two different ways today and neither one is "does this bar physically hit that bar." `rc_automation.validation` runs entirely **before any transaction opens**, against numbers in a parsed workbook row, not placed geometry — its own module docstring states the boundary outright (`validation.py:1-28`): three severities, and `SEVERITY_ERROR` already fires for "overlapping bars" (`validation.py:315`, `"bars would overlap.".format(spacing, diameter)`), but that check is pure arithmetic on one schedule row's own spacing vs. its own diameter — one bar set checked against itself, never against a *different* element's cage, and never against what actually got built. The already-logged Rebar Cover Auditor (2026-09-15) reads real placed geometry, but only ever one distance: bar-to-face, for code-minimum cover. The already-logged Lap & Starter-Bar Continuity Auditor (2026-09-14) also reads real placed geometry, but only within *one continuous vertical run* of the same column's own main bars across a storey joint. Neither one, nor anything else in the repo, ever asks whether *two different elements'* cages occupy the same space where they meet — a footing's top mesh sitting exactly where a column's main-bar cage lands on it, a beam's bottom bars threading past a column's ties at the joint, two independently-run RC Automation passes whose footings share a corner. That gap is not hypothetical for this specific codebase: RC Automation places real footing reinforcement today (`rebar_run.py:118-258`, `_plan_one_footing`/`place_footing`), but per the already-logged Column Reinforcement Placer idea (2026-09-16), it places **zero column bars** — `CRIT-3` in `todo-list.md:98-110` says so outright — so every column cage in a model touched by this toolkit today is hand-modelled, landing on a footing mesh the tool placed automatically, by a different person, at a different time, with no check that the two ever agree on where they can both fit. The raw material for the check already exists, unused for this purpose: `anongee_toolkit.structural.rebar_geometry.line_curves()` (`rebar_geometry.py:66-88`) already turns every bar into an exact world-space centreline `Curve` list before it is placed, and `rebar_spec.BarSpec` already carries `diameter_mm` alongside `points` on every single bar (`rebar_spec.py:172-178`) — centreline plus diameter is exactly what a bar-to-bar proximity test needs, and nothing downstream of `line_curves()` ever asks a second bar's curves the same question. For bars already in the model regardless of who placed them, the real Revit API already used elsewhere in the repo for reading rebar back — `RebarHostData.GetRebarsInHost()` (`rebar_factory.py:552-570`, `existing_stamped_rebar`) — only returns bars hosted *by* one element, so it cannot see a neighbouring host's bars at all; the native per-bar geometry call this idea actually needs, `Rebar.GetCenterlineCurves(...)`, is never called anywhere in the repo (`grep -rn "GetCenterlineCurves" AnonGee.extension` returns nothing). And the broad-phase filter this idea needs to avoid an all-pairs bar-curve scan across a whole model already exists, shared, tested and almost entirely unused: `anongee_toolkit.revit.geometry.bounding_boxes_overlap(bb1, bb2, tol=0.08)` (`geometry.py:40`) is exported from both `anongee_toolkit/__init__.py:108` and `anongee_toolkit/revit/__init__.py:13`, exercised only by the toolkit's own self-test (`tools/verify_toolkit.py:672-677`) — and its one real production caller is not even this shared function: `BIMGeneration.pushbutton/core.py:369` hand-rolls its *own* private copy, `_bounding_boxes_overlap(box_a, box_b, tolerance=-0.05)`, at a different default tolerance than the shared one, to flag column/beam box overlap during CAD import (`core.py:490`) — the exact "one function, two silently-diverging implementations" pattern the already-logged Shared Parameter GUID Doctor (2026-09-18) documents for parameter matching now sitting in the geometry helpers too. On the market, generic clash tools (Navisworks Clash Detective, Solibri) are the wrong shape for this specific question: a column and the beam framing into it are *supposed* to overlap as solids, so whole-element clash rules flag every real joint in the building as a hard clash and need heavy manual rule-tuning before they're usable at all, and neither tool understands "bar," only "element" — a beam's bottom-bar cage physically fouling a column's tie set reads to them as the beam-vs-column clash everyone already suppressed. Paid, seat-licensed rebar-specific detailing suites (AGACAD Rebar for Revit, Naviate Structure) do include a bar-level clash pass, sold as one feature inside a much larger bundle; nothing free checks bar-to-bar congestion at a structural joint using the exact centreline-plus-diameter data this repo's own rebar pipeline already computes for every bar it plans.

**Description:**
`Rebar Clash Checker.pushbutton`, a new sibling to `Rebar Cover Auditor.pushbutton` and `Lap & Starter-Bar Continuity Auditor.pushbutton` — same audience, same modeless-checklist shell, one more question about the same reinforcement:

1. **Scope** — whole model, active view or pre-selection of hosts (`OST_StructuralColumns`, `OST_StructuralFraming`, `OST_StructuralFoundation`, `OST_Walls`); a joint-radius setting (mm) controls how far past each host's own bounding box the scan looks for a neighbour's bars.
2. **Broad phase** — collect every rebar-bearing host in scope, expand each host's `BoundingBoxXYZ` by the joint radius, and pair up hosts whose expanded boxes overlap via `anongee_toolkit.revit.geometry.bounding_boxes_overlap()` — the shared helper finally given a real caller instead of only `tools/verify_toolkit.py` and a hand-rolled duplicate in `BIMGeneration`. Same-host pairs are skipped outright: that is what Rebar Cover Auditor already checks.
3. **Narrow phase** — for each surviving host pair, pull every placed `Rebar`/`RebarInSystem` element on each host (`FilteredElementCollector(doc).OfClass(Rebar).OwnedByHost(host_id)`-equivalent, alongside the toolkit's own `existing_stamped_rebar` pattern for tool-placed bars), read each bar's real centreline via `Rebar.GetCenterlineCurves(adjustForSelfIntersection=True, ...)` and its diameter via `RebarBarType.BarDiameter`, and test every bar from host A against every bar from host B with a 3D segment-to-segment closest-distance check; a pair closer than the sum of their radii (plus a configurable clear-gap tolerance) is a hit.
4. **Report** — one row per clashing bar pair: both host ids/names, both bar roles (main/tie/stirrup/mesh, from the same `ROLE_*` vocabulary `rebar_spec`/`rebar_factory` already use), the overlap depth, a jump-to-element action that selects both bars and zooms the joint into view; severity split the same `Error`/`Warning` way `validation.py` already uses — an outright physical overlap is `Error`, a clearance below a configurable minimum but not yet overlapping is `Warning`.
5. **Report only** — this tool never moves or deletes a bar; it is read-and-flag, matching Rebar Cover Auditor's own posture, since the fix (reroute, restagger, or re-run RC Automation with adjusted cover/spacing) is a modelling decision, not one this tool should make silently.

**Implementation:**
- New `anongee_toolkit.structural.rebar_clash` module: `host_pairs_in_scope(docs, hosts, joint_radius_mm)` (wraps `bounding_boxes_overlap`), `bars_on_host(doc, host)` (native `FilteredElementCollector` + `Rebar`/`RebarInSystem`, filtered to elements actually hosted on the given element), `segment_distance(curve_a, curve_b)` (3D closest-point-between-two-line-segments, run per curve-pair from each bar's `GetCenterlineCurves()` output), `find_clashes(pairs, clear_gap_mm)` returning one record per hit.
- Reuses `rebar_geometry.to_xyz`/`line_curves`'s existing unit-conversion posture (mm in, feet out to the API and back) rather than a second conversion path.
- Same modeless checklist window shell (`anongee_toolkit.ui.hostwnd`/`checklist`/`theme`) and jump-to-element selection every other batch auditor in the repo already runs on; own `bundle.yaml`/`README.md`.
- Fixes the tolerance duplication found while researching this idea as a small, separate first commit: repoint `BIMGeneration.pushbutton/core.py:369`'s `_bounding_boxes_overlap` at the shared `anongee_toolkit.revit.geometry.bounding_boxes_overlap`, or explicitly document why BIMGeneration's `-0.05` default differs from the shared module's `0.08` if it turns out to be deliberate — right now nothing says which.

**Future update:**
- "Since last run" diff mode — flag only a clash newly introduced since the last check (a bar added, a cover changed, a re-run of RC Automation), the same re-run/diff shape already logged as a future item for Type Matrix Generator, Sheet Issue Kit and the Sleeve Coordinator.
- Once the already-logged Column Reinforcement Placer ships and RC Automation starts placing column mains/ties itself, run this checker automatically as a post-placement step on the new cage against whatever footing/beam bars already sit at its base — catching a congested joint the moment automation creates it, not on a later manual audit pass.
- Extend the host-pair scope to the already-logged Sleeve & Opening Coordinator's MEP-vs-structure clash data, so one joint report can eventually show "steel-vs-steel" and "steel-vs-sleeve" congestion side by side instead of two separate tools looking at the same joint.
- A minimum-clear-gap config per bar-role pair (main-vs-main needs more clearance than tie-vs-mesh), read from the same per-project override posture `slab_convert_config.json`/`column_lap_config.json` already establish, rather than one flat tolerance for every kind of bar meeting.

## 2026-09-21 10:40 UTC

**Idea name:** Naming Convention Auditor — walk every Column, Beam, Footing, Slab and Stair type and schedule mark already in the model, recompute what the house convention's own pattern engine says each one should be called, and flag every disagreement, closing the one-directional gap in a rule engine this repo has so far only ever used to write, never to check

**Potential:**
The toolkit already has a full, project-editable house naming convention — `PATTERNS` in `anongee_toolkit/rc_automation/naming_sheet.py:30-40` (`"{b} X {h}"` for a column, `"{l} x {w} x {t}mm"` for a footing, `"{prefix}{n}"` for every schedule mark), overridable from a workbook's own `NAMING` sheet via `naming_sheet.parse()`, rendered deterministically through `naming_sheet.render(patterns, key, **values)` (`naming_sheet.py:173-189`). `grep -rn "naming_sheet.render" AnonGee.extension` finds exactly three call sites, and only two ever touch Revit: `commands/_sheets.py:63` (naming a **type about to be created**) and `commands/_marks.py:89` (naming a **mark about to be written**); the third, `commands/naming.py:95,125`, only regenerates the `NAMING` sheet's own documentation column and writes nothing to a model. There is no fourth call site anywhere that takes a type or instance **already in the model** and checks its current name against what `render()` would produce from that same element's own live dimensions — the engine has a write path and a describe-itself path, and no read-and-compare path at all. This is not a cosmetic gap: `typemaker.choose()` (`anongee_toolkit/structural/typemaker.py:70-109`) documents, in the project owner's own words from 2026-08-27, that *"the house name is the identity"* — when RC Automation looks for a type to reuse, a same-sized type under any other name is refused ("Deliberately no further passes... a type of the right size under another name is *not* this type", lines 106-109), and a **new** type is created instead. So a footing that drifted from the current convention (hand-renamed, imported via `cad2bim`, or left over from before a house-convention change) does not just look inconsistent in a schedule — every later RC Automation run silently manufactures a second, differently-named type of the identical size next to it, which is exactly the duplicate-type bloat the already-logged Type Consolidator (2026-09-13 00:39) has to clean up *after* the fact, on a schedule this repo's own naming engine could catch *before* it happens. On the market, no generic tool could ever check this: the convention is per-project and workbook-defined (`{b} X {h}` today, anything else tomorrow), so only code that already reads the same `NAMING` sheet — which exists nowhere outside this repo — can compute what "correct" even means here; paid whole-discipline QA suites (Naviate Structure, Ideate-class checkers) audit against fixed, generic naming templates, never a project's own live, Excel-editable pattern table.

**Description:**
`Naming Auditor.pushbutton`, sibling to `Bulk Rename.pulldown` in `Essential.panel/Bulk Tool.stack/`, since it is a batch check-then-fix tool for the same daily audience:

1. **Scope + convention source** — whole model, active view or pre-selection, restricted to the five `PATTERNS`-wired kinds `naming_sheet.WIRED` already names (`column`, `beam`, `footing`, `slab`, `stair`, plus the three `*_mark` keys); an optional workbook path re-uses `naming_sheet.parse()` so a project mid-transition to a new convention audits against its **current** rule, not a hardcoded default — same "optional workbook, defaults otherwise" posture `naming.py:20` already documents.
2. **Type pass** — for every `FamilySymbol` in a wired category, read its own governing dimensions the same way `typemaker.size()`/`_set_dimension()` (`typemaker.py:250-269`) already resolve them from a symbol's own parameters, feed those values into `naming_sheet.render(patterns, key, **values)`, and fold-compare (`naming_sheet._fold`, `naming.fold`) the result against the type's actual `Element.Name`.
3. **Mark pass** — for every placed instance, recompute the expected mark the same shape `_marks.py:89` already calls (`prefix` + running `n`), compare against the instance's current `ALL_MODEL_MARK`/`S_ScheduleMark` value.
4. **Report table** — one row per mismatch: category, element/type id, current name, expected name, a jump-to-element action; sortable, and grouped by type so ten drifted instances of one type show as one row to fix, not ten.
5. **Batch fix** — ticked rows only. A type rename goes through `typemaker.named_sibling`/`sanitise` (`typemaker.py:34-67`) rather than a raw `Element.Name =`, because this repo has already hit and solved "two types in one family can't share a name" once (`Duplicate` raising when the target name is taken, `named_sibling`'s whole reason for existing) and a second, independent solve is the risk worth skipping. A mark fix writes the parameter directly, the same read-only-checked `get_Parameter(...).Set(...)` shape `typemaker.set_type_mark` (`typemaker.py:272-286`) already uses.
6. **Export** — `openpyxl`, same path every other batch tool in the repo already writes its report through.

**Implementation:**
- New module `anongee_toolkit.rc_automation.naming_audit`: `expected_type_name(patterns, key, symbol)` (wraps `typemaker.size`'s own parameter-name tables plus `naming_sheet.render`) and `expected_mark(patterns, key, prefix, n)` (mirrors `_marks.py:89`'s call shape exactly, so "what a mark should be" is computed identically whether a job places it fresh or an audit checks it after the fact).
- Collector: one `FilteredElementCollector(doc).OfClass(FamilySymbol)` pass per category in `naming_sheet.WIRED`, paired with one instance pass per category for marks.
- Fix path reuses `typemaker.named_sibling`/`sanitise`/`set_type_mark` rather than re-implementing Revit's type-uniqueness handling a second time.
- Same modeless checklist window shell (`anongee_toolkit.ui.hostwnd`/`checklist`/`theme`) and `openpyxl` export every other batch tool already runs on; own `bundle.yaml`/`README.md`.
- Optional workbook input reuses `rc_automation.excel_engine.read_grid` + `naming_sheet.parse()` exactly as `commands/naming.py` already does, so "read the project's convention" is not a second implementation of the first.

**Future update:**
- Wire as its own bridge command (`naming.audit`), registered in `commands/__init__.py:register_all()` beside the existing `naming.refresh`, so a pre-issue check can run headless through the same bridge `levels.sync`/`grids.sync` already use, per the already-logged Grid Sync (2026-09-19 05:41) posture for exposing a bridge-only capability as a pushbutton (and here, the reverse: exposing a pushbutton capability to the bridge).
- Run the audit automatically as a pre-flight step inside RC Automation itself, so a run reports "3 types will duplicate because their current name doesn't match the house convention" before it places anything, instead of the duplicate simply appearing.
- Extend `PATTERNS`/the audit to Sheets, Views and Grids (ISO 19650 / BEP-style rules) once column/beam/footing/slab/stair prove the audit-then-fix loop, giving the already-logged Universal Renamer (2026-09-11 14:30) a validation pass its own token-substitution engine has no way to check itself against.

## 2026-09-21 05:38 UTC

**Idea name:** Reinforcement Density Heatmap — live kg/m³ steel-to-concrete ratio per column, beam, footing, slab and wall, painted straight onto the model as a graded colour overlay instead of buried in a schedule column, finally wiring the toolkit's own dead volume reader to its own dead weight data

**Potential:**
A modeler or a design lead has exactly one way today to sanity-check "is this column over-reinforced, or is that footing suspiciously light" — open the bar-bending schedule, find every bar tagged to that one host, add up the weight by hand, and divide by a volume nobody has actually computed, because Revit's own schedules report length and count, never a host's net concrete volume. On anything past a handful of elements this check simply never happens, so a transfer beam quietly detailed at 400 kg/m³ (visibly congested, unbuildable cover-to-cover) or a footing at 40 kg/m³ (undersized against its own design intent) ships to IFC drawings and only surfaces on-site when a bar-bender says the cage won't fit in the shutter. The two building blocks this needs already exist in this repo and have never been introduced to each other: `get_solid_volume_m3(element)` (`anongee_toolkit/revit/geometry.py:10-37`) walks any element's solids — including nested `GeometryInstance` geometry — to a net m³ figure, is re-exported as public API in `anongee_toolkit/__init__.py:17,107,222` and `anongee_toolkit/revit/__init__.py:13,37`, and has **zero call sites in any `AnonGee.tab/**/script.py`** (confirmed by `grep -rn "get_solid_volume_m3" AnonGee.extension`, whose only hits are the definition and its own two export declarations — the same dead-export finding the already-logged Design Option Comparator (2026-09-21 00:42) made about this exact function, for a different consumer). On the other side, `BBS Generator.pushbutton/core/bar_record.py:15,25,42-43` already computes `unit_weight_kg_per_m`/`total_weight_kg` per bar via `rebar_reader.py:246-247`'s `standard_module.UNIT_WEIGHTS` lookup, and `rebar_reader.py:68-83`'s `_host_member_type()` already resolves the actual host **element object** (`host = doc.GetElement(host_id)`, line 72) for every rebar it reads — but the `BarRecord` constructor at `rebar_reader.py:250-259` only keeps the host's derived `member_name`/`member_type` **strings**, discarding the host `ElementId` itself (the first element of `_host_member_type()`'s return tuple is thrown away with `_` at line 191, and `revit_element_id=rebar.Id.IntegerValue` at line 257 records the *rebar's* own ID, never the host's). So the schedule this repo already generates has a weight number sitting right next to a member name, and a volume function sitting one folder over that could turn that into a ratio — and nothing has ever connected the two, because the one field that would let them line up (the host's `ElementId`) is computed and then dropped on the floor. On the visualization side, the repo's only per-element graphic-override code, `anongee_toolkit/cad2bim/builders/view_filters.py`, colours whole *categories* one fixed tint each via `ParameterFilterElement`+`OverrideGraphicSettings` (`KINDS` tuple, `apply()`) — it has no notion of a computed, per-element, graduated value, and `grep -rn "SetElementOverrides" AnonGee.extension` returns **zero hits anywhere in the repo**, meaning `View.SetElementOverrides(ElementId, OverrideGraphicSettings)` — the one Revit API call that can paint two columns of the same category two different colours based on a number that isn't a stored parameter — has never been called once. RC Automation's own `rc_automation/validation.py` (`_validate_column_rebar`, `_validate_footing_rebar`, `_check_cage_fits`) checks bar counts, diameters, spacing and cage-fit *before* placement, against its own Excel-driven spec — a schema validator for what RC Automation is about to build, not a live geometric ratio read against whatever rebar (native, imported, or RC Automation's own) already sits in the model today. On the market, native Revit reports no density figure at all; paid whole-discipline QA suites (Naviate Structure, Ideate/Sidekick-class model checkers) audit modeling hygiene (naming, warnings, duplicates) but none of them compute or paint a steel-weight-to-concrete-volume ratio; standalone rebar-takeoff add-ins (the closest commercial category to BBS Generator itself) output a weight *table* and stop there — none turn it into a live, colour-coded model overlay.

**Description:**
`Reinforcement Density Heatmap.pushbutton`, sibling to `BBS Generator.pushbutton` in `Dev.panel/col1.stack/` (the stack that already owns the rebar-weight engine this reuses) or cross-referenced from `RC Automation.pushbutton`'s own panel, since both are its natural data sources:

1. **Scope** — pre-selection, active view, or whole model, same three-way picker `ConvertFloor.pushbutton`/`Design Option Comparator` already use, restricted to Column/Beam/Foundation/Floor/Wall — exactly the five categories `rebar_reader.py:27-30`'s `_HOST_CAT_MAP` already recognises as rebar hosts, so scoping never invents a new category list.
2. **Aggregate weight per host** — for each `Rebar`/`RebarInSystem` in scope, `GetHostId()` (the same call `_host_member_type()` already makes) groups bars by host `ElementId` instead of by the derived name string BBS Generator settles for today; per-bar weight reuses `rebar_reader.py`'s own `total_length_mm × standard_module.UNIT_WEIGHTS[diameter_mm] / 1000.0` formula rather than re-deriving a second one, so a density figure and a BBS Generator export always agree on what one bar weighs.
3. **Volume per host** — `get_solid_volume_m3(host)`, this function's first production call anywhere in the repo, one per host element (not per bar), cached per `ElementId` for the run.
4. **Ratio + band** — `density_kg_m3 = total_bar_weight_kg / (volume_m3 or float("inf"))` (zero-volume hosts report "no geometry" rather than a divide-by-zero), bucketed into a 5-stop band (configurable per member type, since a footing's sane range differs from a column's — e.g. Column: light <100, typical 100–200, heavy 200–300, congested >300 kg/m³) against a starting default table a project can override.
5. **Live table** — one row per host: category, mark/name, weight, volume, density, band — sortable, worst/most-congested first, same posture the already-logged Rebar Cover Auditor (2026-09-16 00:39) established for a different per-bar metric.
6. **Paint** — one click applies `View.SetElementOverrides(host.Id, settings)` per host in the active (or every open) view, `settings` built the same way `cad2bim/builders/view_filters.py:_overrides()` already builds a solid-fill colour override, just one call per element instead of one filter per category — the repo's first per-element, value-graduated paint rather than a fixed per-category one. A legend panel shows the band-to-colour key. A second click clears every override this run applied (element IDs kept in memory for the session), leaving nothing behind the way a filter-based approach would.
7. **Export** — same `openpyxl` path `BBS Generator.pushbutton`/`Export Schedule.pushbutton` already use, one row per host with weight/volume/density/band, so a QA pass leaves a documented record alongside the visual one.

**Implementation:**
- New `anongee_toolkit.structural.reinforcement_density` module: `bars_by_host(doc, categories)` — a single `FilteredElementCollector(doc).OfClass(Rebar)`/`.OfClass(RebarInSystem)` pass grouped by `GetHostId()` — and `density_for_host(host, bars, standard_module)` combining `get_solid_volume_m3(host)` with the existing per-bar weight formula; this is the first module in the repo to key rebar weight by host `ElementId` rather than by the display-only name/mark string `rebar_reader.py` produces today.
- Colour-band lookup, `band_for(density, member_type, table)`, ships a sane default table per member type but reads a project override the same way BBS Generator already lets a project pick its bending-table standard, so the bands are a starting point, not a hardcoded code rule.
- Paint helper, `anongee_toolkit.revit.graphics.paint_by_value(view, host_ids_and_colours)` (new — `revit/views.py` today only enumerates eligible views via `get_eligible_views()`, it has no override helper of any kind), wrapping `View.SetElementOverrides` with the same `<Solid fill>` pattern lookup `view_filters.py:_solid_fill()` already does, so both tools' colours render identically rather than looking like two different systems.
- Reuses `standard_module.UNIT_WEIGHTS` from whichever `BBS Generator.pushbutton/standards/*.py` module the project already has selected, imported rather than duplicated, so a unit-weight table only ever lives in one place.
- Same modeless window shell (`anongee_toolkit.ui.hostwnd`/`checklist`/`theme`) and `openpyxl` export path every other batch tool in the repo already runs on; own `bundle.yaml`/`README.md` per the root README's versioned-tool layout.

**Future update:**
- Feed a confirmed-congested host straight into the already-logged Rebar Cover Auditor (2026-09-16 00:39) and Lap & Starter-Bar Continuity Auditor (2026-09-14 15:39) as a combined "why is this member flagged" QA package, one host, three checks, one report.
- Per-storey or per-zone roll-up (average/total density across every column on a level) for a design-stage sanity check before individual bars are even placed, using RC Automation's own planned rebar quantities instead of placed `Rebar` elements.
- A "target density" input per member (from a structural design output) so the band becomes pass/fail against that member's own design intent instead of a generic typical-range table.
- Extend `paint_by_value()` into a small shared helper other future tools can reuse for any other computed-value overlay (the already-logged Column Stack Auditor's (2026-09-13 05:39) rotation-drift or the Analytical-Physical Alignment Auditor's (2026-09-17 10:45) offset distance would both paint the same way), rather than every future QA tool reinventing its own `OverrideGraphicSettings` call.

## 2026-09-21 00:42 UTC

**Idea name:** Design Option Scheme Comparator & Promoter — per-option concrete-volume and element-count comparison across a model's Option Sets, with a one-click promote that deletes the rejected scheme and dissolves the Option Set in one rollback-safe transaction, making the toolkit's own orphaned solid-volume reader do its first real job

**Potential:**
Every structural team carrying two or three alternative schemes into a design review — a different transfer-beam layout, a wider vs. narrower column grid, precast vs. cast-in-place — models them as native Revit **Design Options** inside one Option Set so the rest of the team can toggle between them in the same file, but Revit gives zero built-in way to *compare* them: no side-by-side quantity delta, no "which option uses less concrete" summary, nothing beyond eyeballing two 3D views back to back. Once a scheme wins, "promoting" it is a fully manual, error-prone slog with no undo-safe shortcut — hand-select and delete every element in the losing option(s), then dissolve the Option Set itself via Manage → Design Options, hoping nothing outside the set (a dimension, a tag, a schedule filter) still silently referenced the geometry that just vanished. This repo has touched the Design Option API exactly once, and only as a guard rail, never a feature: `ConvertFloor.pushbutton/script.py:1182-1186` calls `DB.DesignOption.GetActiveDesignOptionId(doc)` purely to **block** a conversion when the picked element sits in an inactive option ("in design option 'X' - activate it first"), reads `el.DesignOption.Id`/`el.DesignOption.Name` for that one message, and does nothing else with the API — confirmed by `grep -rn "DesignOption" AnonGee.extension --include=*.py`, which returns those three lines and nothing else in the whole extension outside vendored libraries; `grep -rn "OptionSet"` across the entire tree returns **zero hits**, so nothing has ever enumerated an Option Set, compared two options, or written to one. Meanwhile the exact quantity engine this comparison needs already exists and is already a documented, first-class export of the shared toolkit: `get_solid_volume_m3(element)` (`anongee_toolkit/revit/geometry.py:10-37`) walks an element's `Options`/`Solid`/`GeometryInstance` geometry, including nested family instances, and returns net volume in m³, failing safe to `0.0` on any geometry error rather than raising — and it is re-exported by name in both `anongee_toolkit/__init__.py:17,107,222` and `anongee_toolkit/revit/__init__.py:13,37` as public API, yet has **zero call sites in any `AnonGee.tab/**/script.py`** (confirmed by `grep -rn "get_solid_volume_m3" AnonGee.extension`, whose only hits are the definition and its own two export declarations). Its neighbor in the same file, `bounding_boxes_overlap()`, is equally unused by any pushbutton — `BIMGeneration.pushbutton/core.py:369,490` reimplements a private `_bounding_boxes_overlap()` from scratch instead of importing the shared one, a second small sign this geometry module was built ahead of anything that actually calls it. This is a distinct gap from the already-logged **Pour Break Manager** (2026-09-13 05:39), whose own volume read is scoped to a modeler-drawn pour boundary *within* one option and whose write-up says no volume helper exists "anywhere in `anongee_toolkit/structural/`" — true for that folder, but `revit/geometry.py` sits one level up and is the function this idea would make the first real caller of. On the market, native Revit shows no quantity delta between Design Options at all; Ideate Explorer/Sidekick and Naviate/Symetri's Model Checker audit *existing* model health but never compare or promote Design Options, and a genuine option-comparison workflow otherwise lives only inside full paid 5D/cost-estimating suites (Assemble, CostX) that require a separate license and a model export — nothing free does a same-session, Revit-native compare-then-commit on Option Sets.

**Description:**
`Design Option Comparator.pushbutton` (new tool, `Essential.panel/StrucBIM.stack`, sibling to `ConvertFloor.pushbutton`/`DrawFloor.pushbutton` — the one existing precedent for this API surface, and the stack that already owns "read live structural geometry, act in batch" tools) runs in one window:

1. **List Option Sets** — `FilteredElementCollector(doc).OfClass(DB.DesignOptionSet).ToElements()`, one row per set, expandable to its member options (`DesignOptionSet.Options`, each a `DesignOption` with `.Name`/`.IsPrimary`), the repo's first-ever enumeration of this API surface rather than the single activation-guard read `ConvertFloor.pushbutton` already does.
2. **Scope the compare** — pick categories to include (Structural Columns, Framing, Foundations, Floors/Slabs, Walls by default — the same categories the rest of this toolkit already treats as structural), same three-way whole-model/active-view/selection picker `ConvertFloor.pushbutton` already offers, here constrained to elements whose `DesignOption` falls inside the picked set.
3. **Compute per-option quantities** — for every option in the set, `FilteredElementCollector(doc)` filtered to that option's elements in the picked categories, summing `get_solid_volume_m3()` per category (concrete volume being the number a structural reviewer actually wants) alongside a plain element count per category — the first production call this shared function has ever had.
4. **Preview checklist** (`anongee_toolkit.ui.checklist.CheckList`, `anongee_toolkit.ui.hostwnd`/`theme`, the same shell every other multi-step tool in the repo runs on) — a side-by-side table, one column per option: total volume, per-category breakdown, element count, and which option is currently `IsPrimary`; a single radio-style pick selects the option to **promote**, defaulting to none selected so nothing is deletable by accident.
5. **Promote** — one `TransactionGroup` (the same rollback-safe wrapper `ConvertFloor.pushbutton`/`JoinPriority.pushbutton`/RC Automation's own placement pipeline already use): delete every element belonging to every option in the set *other than* the picked one, then dissolve the Option Set itself, folding the surviving option's elements back into the main model the way Revit's own "make primary and delete others" behavior works when done by hand — except reviewed and committed in one step instead of a manual multi-dialog slog.
6. **Report** — per-option volume/count table plus the promote outcome (elements deleted, Option Set dissolved), exportable via the same `openpyxl` path `Export Schedule.pushbutton`/`BBS Generator.pushbutton` already use, so a design-review decision leaves a documented before/after record instead of just a changed model.

**Implementation:**
- New `anongee_toolkit.revit.design_options` module: `list_option_sets(doc)` wrapping `FilteredElementCollector(doc).OfClass(DB.DesignOptionSet)`, `elements_in_option(doc, option, categories)` filtering a category-scoped collector by each element's `DesignOption.Id` matching the target option's `Id` (the exact property `ConvertFloor.pushbutton/script.py:1183` already reads one element at a time, generalized to a whole-option collection pass).
- Volume/count aggregation calls `anongee_toolkit.revit.geometry.get_solid_volume_m3()` per element, summed per category per option — this module's first real caller, promoting it from a documented-but-dead export to production code; failures already return `0.0` rather than raising, so one bad solid never blanks an option's whole total.
- Promote step wraps `Document.Delete(ElementId)` per losing-option element inside the same `TransactionGroup` shape `ConvertFloor.pushbutton` already commits its multi-step conversions through, followed by the API's own Option Set removal call so the set itself doesn't linger empty after its losing members are gone.
- Same modeless window shell (`anongee_toolkit.ui.hostwnd`/`checklist`/`theme`) and `openpyxl` report path every other batch tool in the repo already runs on; own `bundle.yaml`/`README.md` per the root README's versioned-tool layout.
- Reuses `ConvertFloor.pushbutton`'s own `el_name()`/`eid_val()` helpers for consistent element-naming and `ElementId` comparison rather than a second implementation of either.

**Future update:**
- Extend the per-option comparison beyond volume/count to cost, once a project's own material cost-per-m³ table is known (feeding the same `openpyxl` report row), turning "which scheme is smaller" into "which scheme is cheaper."
- A non-destructive "export comparison only" mode that produces the side-by-side report without ever promoting, for design-review packages where the decision isn't final yet.
- Nested Option Set support — a set containing an option that itself hosts a secondary Option Set (a scheme choice within a scheme choice) — once a project actually needs one, rather than assuming every set is flat.
- Feed a confirmed promote straight into **Sheet Issue Kit**'s (2026-09-15 15:40) since-last-run diff, so a design-option decision shows up as "what changed since the last issue" the same way a standards sync or a sheet re-export already would.

## 2026-09-20 15:41 UTC

**Idea name:** Standards Diff & Selective Transfer — per-item comparison and checklist-gated push of line styles, line patterns, fill patterns, materials, view filters and view templates from one model into another, closing the exact all-or-nothing, no-preview blind spot native Transfer Project Standards has always shipped with

**Potential:**
Every multi-project BIM team eventually needs to push "office standards" — line styles, hatch patterns, materials, view filters, view templates — from a maintained master/template file into a live, in-progress project, or from one project into a sibling one so drawings read the same across a job. Revit's own answer is the **Transfer Project Standards** dialog: a flat list of category checkboxes (Line Styles, Materials, Filters, View Templates, and so on), each an all-or-nothing boolean, with **no per-item diff and no preview** — a modeler cannot see *which* line styles differ or *what* a material's parameters will become before clicking OK, and on any name collision Revit's own long-standing, still-current behavior is to silently overwrite the project's copy with the source's, no matter which one was actually customized for that job. The cost of getting it wrong is invisible until a sheet plots differently or a schedule pulls the wrong material cost, by which point the transfer that caused it is long since buried in the undo stack. Nothing in this repo touches that gap today. `Bulk Rename.pulldown`/`Bulk Delete.pulldown` (`Rename LineStyles.pushbutton`, `Rename FillPatterns.pushbutton`, `Rename LinePatterns.pushbutton`, and their three `Delete *` siblings) are real, working batch tools for exactly these three categories — but every one of them, by its own single-file `TARGET = "linestyle"`-style config line (e.g. `Rename LineStyles.pushbutton/script.py:6`) and its single `doc = __revit__.ActiveUIDocument.Document` (`script.py:31`), reads and writes **one already-open document only**; `Delete LineStyles.pushbutton/script.py:80-81`'s `doc.Settings.Categories.get_Item(BuiltInCategory.OST_Lines).SubCategories` walk is the same single-document accessor every sibling button repeats, never a second file to compare against. `anongee_toolkit.structural.inventory.gather()` (`structural/inventory.py:33-48`) already proves the harder half of this problem is solved in this codebase: it walks a whole document once, section by section (`_model`, `_categories`, `_families`, `_parameters`, `_datum`, `_views`), each section wrapped in its own `_safe()` so one failing section (a raised `GlobalParametersManager` call, say) never blanks the rest — turning a `.rvt`, in the module's own words, into "plain data" a person (or a diff) can read without Revit open at all. But its five sections stop short of exactly the categories Transfer Project Standards moves: no line styles, no line/fill patterns, no materials, no view filters, no view templates anywhere in `gather()`'s output. And no tool anywhere in this repo — not `inventory.py`, not the Bridge, not RC Automation — ever calls `Application.OpenDocumentFile()`; every script in `AnonGee.tab/**` operates on the single active document its host session already has open, so comparing *two* models (the office master and the live project) is a Revit API surface this toolkit has never once exercised. On the market, per-item standards diffing exists only inside expensive whole-suite tools (Ideate BIM Link's "Compare/Transfer" surface, or a Navisworks/BIM 360-tier model-comparison feature) bundled into paid subscriptions well beyond a single selective-transfer button; nothing free does a scoped, previewed, this-item-only push of exactly these six categories between two `.rvt` files.

**Description:**
`Standards Diff.pushbutton` (new tool, `Advance.panel`, sibling to `Parameter Combine.pushbutton` since both are two-document, checklist-driven comparison tools) runs in one window:

1. **Pick the source** — a file browser for a second, closed `.rvt` (the office master/template, or a sibling project) opened read-only in the background via `Application.OpenDocumentFile()`, never touching the active session's document until a transfer is actually confirmed; the active document is always the target.
2. **Pick the categories** — Line Styles, Line Patterns, Fill Patterns, Materials, View Filters, View Templates: a checklist of which of the six to compare this run, since a team pulling only materials shouldn't have to wait on a filter/view-template walk too.
3. **Diff** — for each checked category, collect both documents' items by name (`doc.Settings.Categories.get_Item(BuiltInCategory.OST_Lines).SubCategories` for line styles, the same accessor `Delete LineStyles.pushbutton`/`Rename LineStyles.pushbutton` already use, one call per document instead of one; `FilteredElementCollector(doc).OfClass(FillPatternElement/LinePatternElement/Material/ParameterFilterElement/View).ToElements()` for the rest) and bucket every name into **source-only** (exists in the master, missing here — a candidate to add), **target-only** (exists here, not in the master — left alone, never deleted by this tool), and **in both** (compare the resolved definition — pattern geometry, material's own graphic/identity/appearance parameters, a filter's rule set, a view template's controlled-settings list — and mark **identical** or **differs**).
4. **Preview checklist** (`anongee_toolkit.ui.checklist`, the same `CheckList` component `Type Splitter`'s own preview and `ConvertFloor.pushbutton`'s per-element plan already run on) — one row per source-only or differing item, grouped by category, each differing row expandable into a plain before/after of exactly what changed; every row defaults **unticked** except source-only items, so a modeler transfers only what they actually reviewed and nothing already customized in the target gets silently clobbered by default.
5. **Transfer** — one `TransactionGroup`; for each ticked item, `ElementTransformUtils.CopyElements()` (system items) or a duplicate-and-remap for family-hosted ones, source document into target, colliding names resolved by explicit overwrite (only because the row was ticked, never implicit); view templates carry their controlled-settings list across, not just the name.
6. **Report** — a per-category add/update/skip count and, for every skipped-because-unticked differing item, the specific field left alone — the same `openpyxl` export path `Export Schedule.pushbutton`/`BBS Generator.pushbutton` already use — so a transfer run leaves a record of exactly what a team chose not to touch, not just what it changed.
7. **Close** the source document (never saved, never modified) once the transfer commits or the window closes.

**Implementation:**
- New `anongee_toolkit.revit.standards_diff` module: `open_reference(path)` wrapping `Application.OpenDocumentFile()` with a guard against pointing it at the already-active document or a file already open elsewhere in the session; per-category collectors reusing the exact accessors `Delete */Rename *.pushbutton` already prove (`doc.Settings.Categories` for line styles, `FilteredElementCollector(doc).OfClass(...)` for the rest) but run once per document instead of once total.
- Diff logic modeled directly on `inventory.py`'s own shape: one comparison function per category, each wrapped the way `_safe()` wraps `_categories`/`_families`/`_parameters` (`structural/inventory.py:50-56`) so one category's collector raising (a corrupt filter, an unreadable material asset) never blanks the other five.
- Item-level "what changed" text for materials/filters/view templates reuses `anongee_toolkit.revit.parameters` value-formatting helpers already shared across `Parameter Combine.pushbutton` and `Export Schedule.pushbutton`, rather than a third ad hoc value-to-string function.
- Transfer step wraps `ElementTransformUtils.CopyElements()` per category rather than calling Revit's own `Document.TransferProjectStandards()` wholesale — the entire point being per-item, ticked-only control that the built-in API method, mirroring its own dialog, does not expose.
- Same modeless window shell (`anongee_toolkit.ui.hostwnd`/`progressbar`/`checklist`/`theme`) and `openpyxl` report path every other batch tool in the repo already runs on; source-document lifecycle (`open_reference` → never-saved → `Document.Close(False)`) is the one genuinely new pattern this tool introduces to the codebase.

**Future update:**
- A saved "standards profile" (which categories, which named items, ticked or not) so a recurring monthly pull from the same office master doesn't require re-reviewing every already-accepted row each time — only what changed in the master since the last run.
- Extend the diffed-category list to Object Styles (line weight/color/material per model category, `doc.Settings.Categories` itself rather than just its Lines subcategories) and Keynotes once a project's own naming/keynote-file conventions are known.
- A "reverse-flag" pass: items that differ where the *target's* value looks more like a deliberate project-specific override (a shared parameter's formula referencing a project-only Global Parameter, say) get called out in the preview rather than presented as an ordinary source-wins/target-wins choice, so a modeler doesn't accidentally overwrite a customization the diff itself can already tell was intentional.
- Feed a confirmed transfer's report straight into the same since-last-run diff **Sheet Issue Kit** (2026-09-15 15:40) already proposes for exports, so "what standards changed since the last office sync" becomes as visible as "what sheets changed since the last issue."

## 2026-09-20 10:41 UTC

**Idea name:** Type Splitter — find every Type whose instances have quietly drifted onto different real sizes through an instance-bound dimension parameter, and safely re-house each distinct size onto its own honestly-named Type, undoing exactly the drift `Type Consolidator`'s own mirror-image problem never covers

**Potential:**
A Revit family only reports one true size per Type when every dimension that defines that size is bound at Type scope — but plenty of loaded libraries (legacy office families, a consultant's rectangular-column/beam family, anything authored for "quick resize flexibility") expose at least one governing dimension (width, depth, a haunch, a cover) as an **Instance** parameter instead, precisely so a modeler under deadline can nudge one placed element's size in the Properties palette without hunting the Type Selector for an exact match. Every one of those nudges is invisible in the one place a modeler would notice it: the Type Selector still shows one Type name, `Structural Schedule`s and `BBS Generator.pushbutton`'s own bar-length/quantity output still group by that one Type, and nobody sees that "C1" is actually four different real column sizes until a quantity mismatches a site measurement or a rebar detail is drawn against the wrong section — the *opposite* failure from the one already logged as **Type Consolidator** (2026-09-13 15:39), whose whole premise is "many Types that are secretly one real thing, merge them," with no mention anywhere in its own write-up of the reverse case, one Type that is secretly several real things. This repo's own automated placement path proves the discipline exists and is deliberately protected: `structural/columns.py:218-230`'s `_set_dimension()` and `structural/framing.py:289-299`'s twin both call `symbol.LookupParameter(name)` — writing a computed size onto the **symbol** (the Type), never the placed `instance` — specifically because RC Automation's own author treats a size as a Type-level fact; nothing about that discipline is enforced, or even checked, once a column or beam exists outside RC Automation's own placement run and a modeler edits it by hand afterward, family-instance Properties panel open, typing straight into whichever dimension the family happens to expose as an Instance parameter. `anongee_toolkit.revit.parameters.get_parameter()` (`revit/parameters.py:15-30`) makes this worse to even notice from code: it checks the **instance** first and only falls back to the type, so every value this toolkit's own shared helper would ever report already erases the distinction between "this is what the Type says" and "this instance overrode it" — confirmed, as **Parameter Migrator** (2026-09-12 15:39) already independently found, by the fact that `get_parameter`/`get_parameter_value` currently have zero call sites in any `AnonGee.tab/**/script.py`, so nothing has ever been built against that resolution order to notice what it hides. On the market, "instance override drift" detection exists only as a generic rule inside paid whole-model QA/health-check suites (Ideate Sidekick's model audits, Naviate/Symetri's Model Checker family, both already cited for the *adjacent* Column Stack Auditor and Family Health Auditor ideas above) that flag "instance parameter differs from type default" as one line item among dozens, with no structural-specific action attached to the finding; none of them then safely duplicates a correctly-named replacement Type and reassigns the drifted instances onto it the way this toolkit's own `typemaker.py` already knows how to.

**Description:**
`Type Splitter.pushbutton` (new tool, `Essential.panel/Bulk Tool.stack`, the direct opposite-number sibling of the already-logged **Type Consolidator**) runs in one window:

1. **Scope** — pick one or more categories (Structural Columns and Structural Framing first, since `structural/columns.py`/`structural/framing.py` already prove which dimension parameter names each family convention uses; Structural Foundations and loadable Walls/generic families follow once a firm's own family list is known), same whole-model / active-view / selection three-way picker `ConvertFloor.pushbutton` already offers.
2. **Pick the watch parameters** — a small list of dimension-shaped parameter names to monitor (seeded from the same names `_set_dimension()`'s own `names` argument already tries in `columns.py`/`framing.py` — width/depth/breadth spellings — editable per firm), because only a modeler-nameable subset of parameters are ever meant to define a Type's real size; watching every parameter would flag legitimate per-instance fields (Mark, Comments, an actual instance property) as false drift.
3. **Group and detect drift** — for every Type in scope, collect its instances and read each watch parameter directly off the **instance** (bypassing `get_parameter()`'s type-fallback on purpose, the opposite need from every other tool that already uses it) via `anongee_toolkit.revit.parameters.get_parameter_value(..., as_string=False)`; a Type whose instances don't all agree on every watch parameter is a **drifted Type**, and its instances cluster into one group per distinct value combination.
4. **Preview checklist** (`anongee_toolkit.ui.checklist`) — one row per drifted Type, expandable into one sub-row per distinct-value cluster: instance count, the resolved dimensions, and a **proposed new Type name** built the same way RC Automation's own naming sheet convention names a duplicated Type (`structural/typemaker.py`'s `sanitise()`/`_candidates()`, e.g. `C1` splitting into `C1 (230x450)` / `C1 (230x600)` when no house-naming pattern applies) — the cluster already matching the Type's own current values (the "correct" majority) is pre-unticked and left alone; every other cluster is ticked to split by default.
5. **Split** — one `TransactionGroup`, per ticked cluster: `structural/typemaker.duplicate(doc, base=<the drifted Type's Symbol>, preferred=<proposed name>)` (the exact function already proven from inside RC Automation's own placement pipeline, `structural/typemaker.py:170-226` — same clash-numbering, same "existing type of the right size reused instead of a second copy" logic, same reported *why* on every fallback), then the same `_set_dimension()`-style write to push each watch parameter's clustered value onto the new Type, `element.ChangeTypeId()` per instance in the cluster (the reassignment call **Type Consolidator** already proposes reusing from `structural/stairs.py:224,339`), and finally clearing the now-redundant instance-level override so the value reads cleanly from the Type going forward, not from a leftover instance value that now happens to match.
6. **Report** — Types split / new Types created / instances reassigned / clusters left alone as already-correct, exportable via the same `openpyxl` path `Export Schedule.pushbutton`/`BBS Generator.pushbutton` already use — a "Type Selector count before/after" line, mirroring Type Consolidator's own report shape but going the other direction.

**Implementation:**
- New `anongee_toolkit.revit.type_drift` module: instance-level watch-parameter read via `anongee_toolkit.revit.parameters.get_parameter_value` (its first direct, user-facing call site per **Parameter Migrator**'s own already-logged finding that none currently exists), clustering instances of one Type by their watch-parameter value tuple.
- New-Type creation and naming reuse `anongee_toolkit.structural.typemaker.duplicate()`/`sanitise()`/`_candidates()` verbatim (`structural/typemaker.py:170-247`) rather than a second implementation of "safely duplicate and name a Type," promoting that module from an RC-Automation-only internal to a second, independent caller.
- Dimension write onto the new Type mirrors `structural/columns.py:218-230`'s `_set_dimension()` pattern (`symbol.LookupParameter(name)`, `IsReadOnly` guard, `mm_to_ft` conversion) generalized to whatever watch-parameter list the config supplies, instead of the two categories' current hard-coded name lists.
- Instance reassignment is `element.ChangeTypeId()`, the same call already proven at `structural/stairs.py:224,339` and proposed for reuse by **Type Consolidator**; override-clearing afterward is `Parameter.Set(...)` back to whatever the type's own resolved value now is (or, where the API allows it, restoring the parameter's "value from type" state) so nothing re-drifts on the very next read.
- `type_split_config.json` alongside `script.py`: per-category watch-parameter name list, minimum instance count before a cluster is worth splitting (a one-off outlier below the threshold is flagged for review, not auto-split), naming pattern fallback when no house-naming sheet applies — mirroring `slab_convert_config.json`'s override-by-config shape.
- Same modeless window shell (`anongee_toolkit.ui.hostwnd`/`progressbar`/`checklist`/`theme`) and `openpyxl` report path every other batch tool in the repo already runs on.

**Future update:**
- A combined **Type Health** run that fires Type Consolidator's merge pass and this tool's split pass back to back over the same scope, since a model can carry both problems at once (duplicate Types on one hand, drifted instances within a Type on the other) and today a modeler would have to know which one to reach for.
- Feed a confirmed split straight into `structural/typemaker.py`'s own naming-sheet lookup once a project's NAMING workbook is loaded, so a split Type's name comes from the house convention `choose()` (`typemaker.py:70-125`) already prefers, instead of the size-suffix fallback used when no naming sheet applies.
- Extend the watch-parameter idea to rebar (`anongee_toolkit.structural.rebar_types`) once its own instance/type parameter split is mapped the same way, catching a bar diameter or spacing hand-edited per instance instead of driven by `RebarBarType`.
- A "lock it down" follow-up option: after a split, flip the watch parameter's family-editor Instance/Type flag itself (where the loaded family allows editing in-session) so the same drift can't recur on the newly split Types — closing the loop from "detect and fix" to "prevent," the same posture already logged as a future step for Shared Parameter GUID Doctor.

## 2026-09-20 05:40 UTC

**Idea name:** Section Batch Generator — one-pass `ViewSection.Create` across every picked column, beam or wall (or one per type), auto-cropped, templated, named and dropped on a sheet, closing a Revit API surface this repo has never once called

**Potential:**
Every structural GA and detail sheet set needs a cut section through each recurring condition — a typical column section, a beam mid-span section, a shear-wall section at each thickness change — and Revit's native path is entirely manual and entirely per-instance: draw a section line in plan, drag the far clip plane and crop region to the right depth and width, rename the view off the default "Section 1/2/3", apply a view template, then place it on a sheet at the right callout/detail number. That is 6-8 fiddly steps, repeated once per column, once per beam, once per wall condition — on a building with 40 column types and a hundred beams that is a full day of pure view-admin with zero design content, and it recurs every time a later revision adds a new column size or wall type that needs its own typical section. A repo-wide search confirms this capability has never been built here: grepping every `script.py` and every `anongee_toolkit` module for `ViewSection` and `ViewSection.Create` returns **zero hits** anywhere outside this idea log — the one adjacent-looking hit, `SectionType` in `Export Schedule.pushbutton/script.py:13,757`, is `ViewSchedule.SectionType.Body` (a schedule table's Body/Header/Summary row group), a same-named but entirely unrelated Revit API class, confirmed by reading the call site (`s.GetTableData().GetSectionData(SectionType.Body)`). The one place a building *section* is even read is `anongee_toolkit/revit/views.py:8-17`'s `get_eligible_views()`, which lists `ViewType.Section` among the view types it filters to for labelling purposes (`revit/views.py:14`) — a read-only enumeration whose only caller today is `API.md`'s own documentation example, per the already-logged **Sheet Set Builder** idea (2026-09-14 10:39), and that tool's own scope is plan views and sheets, never a section or callout view; it explicitly stops at `ViewPlan.Create()` (`AutoLevel.pushbutton/anongee_autolevel/revit_ops.py:483-489`). No pushbutton in the repo has ever called `Document.Create.NewViewSection()`/`ViewSection.CreateSection()`, chosen a `ViewFamilyType` for a section, or computed a `BoundingBoxXYZ`/`Transform` to crop one around an element. On the market, this exact batch — "one section per selected element or per type, auto-cropped, auto-named, on a sheet" — exists only as a narrow feature inside large paid Revit production suites (Ideate BIMLink/Sidekick view tools, CTC's View/Sheet Creator, Naviate's detailing tools), licensed per seat as part of a much broader bundle; Revit's own UI and every free pyRevit extension leave it exactly as manual as described above.

**Description:**
`Section Batch Generator.pushbutton` (new tool, `Essential.panel/StrucBIM.stack`, sibling to `ConvertFloor.pushbutton`/`DrawFloor.pushbutton`/`RoundElementDist.pushbutton` — the stack that already owns "read live structural geometry, act in batch" tools):

1. **Scope** — pre-selection, active view, or whole model, the same three-way picker `ConvertFloor.pushbutton` already offers; filtered to `OST_StructuralColumns`, `OST_StructuralFraming` and structural `Walls` (shear/RC walls), the categories the rest of this toolkit already treats as structural.
2. **One-per-element or one-per-type** — a toggle: "every selected element" (a section per instance, for a small hand-picked set) or "one representative per distinct type" (grouped the same way `Type Consolidator`'s already-logged compound-structure/parameter signature grouping works, one level up — first instance of each type stands in for the section), the common case for a "typical section" sheet where 40 columns reduce to 6 real column types.
3. **Cut plane + crop rule per category** — a small rule table (through element centreline vs. through a picked face, crop depth/width as a multiple of element size or a fixed value, far-clip on/off) with sensible structural defaults, editable per firm and saved to `section_batch_config.json`.
4. **Preview checklist** (`anongee_toolkit.ui.hostwnd`/`checklist`/`progressbar`, the same shell every other multi-step tool in the repo runs on) — one row per element or type: resolved section name, host element, crop dimensions, target view template, status (**will create** / **already exists — skip**, checked the same way `get_eligible_views()` already lets `Sheet Set Builder` check for a matching plan); rows tick/untick before anything is created.
5. **Create** — one `Transaction`: for each ticked row, build the cutting `BoundingBoxXYZ`/`Transform` centred on the element per the rule table, call `ViewSection.CreateSection(doc, section_type_id, box)`, apply the configured view template, rename per the token-template formatter the already-logged **Universal Renamer** idea proposes (falling back to a small standalone formatter if that tool hasn't landed yet, exactly the reuse posture `Sheet Set Builder` already commits to for the same reason); optionally place the new section on a picked sheet as a detail/callout viewport at the next free detail number, reusing `Sheet Set Builder`'s own sheet/titleblock enumeration (`structural/inventory.py:323-359`) rather than a second implementation.
6. **Report** — created / skipped-already-exists / blocked counts, exportable via the same `openpyxl` path `Export Schedule.pushbutton`/`BBS Generator.pushbutton` already use.

**Implementation:**
- New `anongee_toolkit.revit.section_batch` module — the repo's first caller of `ViewSection.CreateSection`/`Document.Create.NewViewSection()`, built the same way every other geometry-touching module here is: read element geometry (bounding box via `Element.get_BoundingBox(None)`, the same call `Type Consolidator`'s and `ConvertFloor.pushbutton`'s own signature helpers already use), compute a section `BoundingBoxXYZ` and `Transform` around it, then create.
- Element scoping and type-grouping reuse `ConvertFloor.pushbutton`'s existing three-way selection picker and `Type Consolidator`'s compound-structure/parameter signature grouping rather than two new implementations of "reduce many elements to one per type."
- Existing-section detection extends `get_eligible_views()` (`revit/views.py:30-56`) with a "already has a section roughly centred on this element" check (bounding-box proximity against existing `ViewSection` origins) — the second real caller of that helper after `Sheet Set Builder`, both promoting it from a documentation-only utility to production code.
- Naming and sheet placement reuse **Universal Renamer**'s token formatter and **Sheet Set Builder**'s titleblock/sheet-number enumeration exactly as those two ideas' own "don't diverge on shared syntax" posture already commits to.
- `section_batch_config.json` alongside `script.py`: per-category cut-plane rule, crop multiplier/fixed size, far-clip default, view template name, sheet-placement default (on/off) — mirroring `slab_convert_config.json`'s override-by-config shape used throughout the repo.
- Same modeless window shell and Excel report path every other batch tool in the repo already runs on; own `bundle.yaml`/`README.md` per the root README's versioned-tool layout.

**Future update:**
- "Since last run" diff mode — flag a type that gained a section since the last build vs. one still missing one, the same re-run/diff shape already logged for Type Matrix Generator, Sheet Issue Kit and the Sleeve Coordinator.
- Detail-view variant: same engine, `ViewType.Detail` instead of `ViewType.Section`, for zoomed callouts (rebar lap detail, base-plate detail) nested inside a section this tool already created.
- Auto re-crop when a hosting element's size changes after a type edit, instead of leaving a stale crop box after a revision.
- Feed the produced section set straight into **Sheet Set Builder**'s own sheet-numbering pass as an additional discipline row, so a full drawing set (plans, sections, sheets) comes out of one continuous run instead of two separate tools handed off by hand.

## 2026-09-20 00:39 UTC

**Idea name:** Spot Elevation Sweep — one-pass, every-corner Spot Elevation/Coordinate placement on footing, pile cap and slab top faces, replacing Revit's pick-one-point-at-a-time native tool

**Potential:**
Every foundation and slab plan needs top-of-concrete (or top-of-footing) levels called out at every corner so a site engineer can set out formwork without opening the model — and Revit's native Spot Elevation tool places exactly one point per click, requiring the modeler to hover with sub-pixel precision over the exact edge or vertex of the right face in a cluttered plan view, then repeat that for every corner of every pad. A 40-footing raft or a stepped foundation plan with 6-8 corners per pad is 250+ individual, error-prone clicks — miss the face by a pixel and Revit either silently snaps to the wrong reference (a neighbouring pad's edge) or refuses the pick outright, and there is no way to batch it, no way to preview what will be tagged before it's placed, and no undo-safe way to redo a whole level's worth after a footing schedule revision moves half the pads. This is pure, repeated annotation drudgery of the exact shape this toolkit already automates elsewhere (`AutoDIM.pushbutton` for extents, `Tag Sweep` — already logged, 2026-09-17 — for element tags) but it has never been pointed at spot annotations: a repo-wide search for `SpotDimension`, `SpotElevation`, `SpotCoordinate`, `NewSpotElevation` and `NewSpotCoordinate` across every `script.py` and every `anongee_toolkit` module turns up exactly one hit, and it is a *read*, not a *write* — `AutoLevel.pushbutton/anongee_autolevel/revit_ops.py:178-229` collects existing `SpotDimension` elements purely to scrape their `Origin.Z` as another source of "what elevation text is already on this sheet" for its own level-labelling scan; nothing in the repo has ever called `Document.NewSpotElevation` or `Document.NewSpotCoordinate` to create one. RC Automation's own footing module makes this worse than it needs to be, not better: `anongee_toolkit/structural/footings.py:1-16`'s own docstring is explicit that pads are modelled as **floors** ("Footings are floors here rather than family instances... a pad that is not rectangular can be *scheduled* instead of approximated by the nearest rectangle it fits inside"), which means the hundreds of pads RC Automation places in one `TransactionGroup` still leave the modeler to spot-elevation every corner of every one of them by hand afterwards, one pick at a time, with zero help from the tool that just created them. On the market, batch spot-elevation placement across a footprint's corners exists only as a narrow utility line item bundled inside large paid Revit add-in suites sold per seat (Naviate's annotation tools, CTC BIM Tools' multi-element annotation utilities); Revit's own UI has no batch path at all, and nothing free reads a slab/footing's own top-face geometry and places a full, collision-checked set of corner spot elevations in one command.

**Description:**
`Spot Elevation Sweep.pushbutton` (sibling to `AutoDIM.pushbutton` in `Essential.panel/Action.stack/`) takes a pre-selection or active-view filter of footings, pile caps, slabs or walls and places a full set of Spot Elevations (or Spot Coordinates, toggled per run) across every element's top face in one command:

1. For each selected element, finds its top-most horizontal planar face (normal ≈ +Z within a tolerance) with a valid geometry `Reference` — the same face-finding approach `AutoDIM.pushbutton` already uses for dimensioning, generalized to pick "topmost" instead of "outermost along an axis."
2. Reads the face's outer boundary loop and reduces it to its corner vertices (for a rectangular pad, 4 points; for an L-shaped or combined footing outline — the exact shape RC Automation's own footings module already supports as a sketched floor — however many corners the sketch has); a face with no clean vertex set (a curved edge, a filleted corner) falls back to a user-configurable "midpoint of each straight segment" mode instead of being silently skipped.
3. Runs a collision pass against every existing `SpotDimension` already in the view within a configurable radius, so re-running the tool after a footing schedule revision doesn't stack duplicate tags on corners that already have one — new points only.
4. Shows the same pre-flight checklist UX used across the toolkit (`anongee_toolkit.ui.checklist.CheckList`): one row per element, corner count, a "will be tagged / already tagged / needs fallback mode" status per row, ticked rows proceed.
5. One `Transaction`, calling `Document.NewSpotElevation` (or `NewSpotCoordinate`) per accepted corner with no leader, referencing the face found in step 1 — batch-created exactly the way a modeler would place them one at a time, just without the per-pick precision hunt.
6. Result report: elements processed, points placed, points skipped as already-tagged, elements that needed fallback mode — same shape as every other batch tool's summary in this repo.

**Implementation:**
- New top-face finder built directly on `AutoDIM.pushbutton/script.py:87-109`'s `planar_faces()`/`extreme_faces()` pair: reuse `planar_faces()` verbatim (it already sets `Options.ComputeReferences = True` and filters to `DB.PlanarFace` instances carrying a non-null `.Reference`, `script.py:90,104`), then replace `extreme_faces()`'s "two outermost along an axis" selection (`script.py:111-127`) with a "single face whose `FaceNormal` is most aligned with global +Z" selection — a small variant of the same dot-product comparison already there, not new geometry math.
- Corner extraction from the winning face's `EdgeLoops`/`GetEdgesAsCurveLoops()`, walking each `CurveLoop` and keeping the start point of every `Line` segment (an `Arc` segment routes that face into the fallback "midpoint of each non-line segment" mode instead of guessing a corner that doesn't exist).
- Collision radius check against `FilteredElementCollector(doc, view.Id).OfClass(SpotDimension)` — the exact same collector `AutoLevel.pushbutton/anongee_autolevel/revit_ops.py:178,187-189` already runs, reused here as a duplicate-guard read rather than a text-scrape source.
- Pre-flight checklist reuses `anongee_toolkit.ui.checklist.CheckList` (`anongee_toolkit/ui/checklist.py`), the same CPython-safe stand-in for `forms.SelectFromList` already used across the toolkit's other multi-row review dialogs, so the new tool's dialog looks and behaves like every sibling batch tool rather than introducing a new UI pattern.
- New `spot_elevation_config.json` alongside `script.py`: `mode` (`"elevation" | "coordinate"`), `collision_radius_mm`, `fallback_mode` (`"segment_midpoint" | "skip"`), `top_face_tolerance_deg`.
- Sits in `Essential.panel/Action.stack/`, next to `AutoDIM.pushbutton` / `Export Schedule.pushbutton` / `Rotate Column.pushbutton`, same `bundle.yaml` layout as its stack-mates.

**Future update:**
- Pull each footing's own top elevation (once RC Automation's placement schedule exposes one directly, rather than only the outline `anongee_toolkit/rc_automation/models.py:450-462`'s `FootingPlacement.outline` field carries today) to cross-check the placed spot value against the design intent and flag a mismatch instead of just annotating whatever the model geometry already says.
- Grid-intersection mode: place Spot Coordinates at every grid intersection over a slab in one pass, feeding off the same grid-geometry read `RoundElementDist.pushbutton/script.py:48-54` and the already-logged **Grid Mark Assistant** (2026-09-13) both already do, instead of only per-element corners.
- Diff/re-run mode: re-scan after a revision and only add spot elevations for corners that are new or moved since the last sweep, tagging the rest as already covered — the same "re-run and diff" shape already logged as a future item for Type Matrix Generator and the Sleeve Coordinator.
- Batch retag mode: select a set of existing Spot Elevations and re-run them onto a replacement face after a footing is regenerated by `Convert Slab` or the already-logged **Footing Combiner** (2026-09-19), instead of leaving orphaned annotations pointing at a deleted element.
