## 2026-09-17 15:41 UTC

**Idea name:** Tag Sweep — collision-aware bulk tagging for structural elements, with automatic leader routing when a clean inline placement would overlap something already on the sheet

**Potential:**
Placing tags is one of the last, most tedious manual steps in every structural plan, and it is the one annotation task this toolkit has never touched at all: a repo-wide grep for `IndependentTag`/`TagOrientation`/`.Tag` inside `AnonGee.extension` turns up zero hits outside third-party library code (`lib/py3/fontTools`, unrelated) — nothing here has ever called the Revit tagging API. Modelers fall back to Revit's native **Tag All Not Tagged**, which places every tag at a fixed offset from each element's own insertion point with no awareness of anything else on the sheet: on a real structural plan — columns on a tight grid, beam tags crowding the same bay, a dimension string running underneath, a grid bubble sitting exactly where the default offset lands — the result is a pile of overlapping tag boxes that then gets dragged apart by hand, tag by tag, leader by leader, for every view on every level, redone in full whenever a revision adds elements. That manual clean-up pass is a recurring hour-plus tax per sheet set that nothing in this repo or the free-tool market addresses: paid add-ins that do collision-aware tag placement exist (CTC's tag/dimension tools, BIM One's annotation utilities, assorted Autodesk App Store "smart tag" add-ins) sold per seat as drafting-automation bundles, but nothing free reads a view's existing annotation and routes new tags around it. The toolkit already has two of the three hard pieces this needs, sitting in tools that never touch tags: `AutoDIM.pushbutton/script.py` already solves "read an element's own in-plane extents and a clean offset point outside its geometry" (`element_frame`, `script.py:70-84`; `planar_faces`/`extreme_faces`, `script.py:87-126`; `bbox_center`, `script.py:146-150`) — built to anchor a dimension line, but the same anchor point is exactly where a tag needs to sit before collision-avoidance nudges it clear; and the already-logged **Dimension Sweep** idea (2026-09-14 00:39) already proposes promoting that same engine into a shared `anongee_toolkit.revit.dimensioning` module and reading `FilteredElementCollector(doc, active_view.Id).OfClass(DB.Dimension)` to avoid stacking a duplicate dimension on a re-run — the read-what's-already-on-the-sheet posture this tool needs too, just never extended to tags, grid bubbles or other tags. The third piece — the already-logged **Grid Mark Assistant** idea (2026-09-13 00:39) — names this exact gap directly in its own future-update list: "once marks are assigned, auto-place independent tags at the resolved intersection point with a configurable leader, closing the loop from computed Mark to drawing annotation in one pass" — a single unbuilt bullet point, not a tool. This closes that loop, but scoped wider than Grid Mark Assistant's own output: it tags whatever Mark (or Type, or any tag-driven parameter) an element already carries, grid-derived or not, so it stands on its own the moment it ships and simply gets better placement input once Grid Mark Assistant exists.

**Description:**
`Tag Sweep.pushbutton` (new tool in `Essential.panel/Action.stack`, sibling to `AutoDIM.pushbutton` and the already-logged `Dimension Sweep.pushbutton` — a third caller of the same promoted geometry engine) runs in one window:

1. **Scope & categories** — active view only (tags, like dimensions, are view-specific — the same constraint `AutoDIM` already enforces via `_NO_DIM_VIEWS`, `script.py:34-40`), tick which categories to sweep: Structural Columns, Structural Framing, Structural Walls, Floors/Foundations — no pre-selection required, run against every untagged element the ticked categories collect in the active view.
2. **Find what's already there** — before placing anything, collects every `IndependentTag` already in the view (bounding box + leader end point), every `Dimension` (`FilteredElementCollector(doc, active_view.Id).OfClass(DB.Dimension)`, the exact read the already-logged Dimension Sweep idea introduces for its own duplicate-guard), and every `Grid`/`Level` bubble location — the "what's already on this sheet" map neither `AutoDIM` nor Revit's own Tag All Not Tagged ever builds.
3. **Untagged-element report** — every element in scope that has no `IndependentTag` referencing it (`IndependentTag.GetTaggedLocalElementIds()` reverse-checked against the collected set) is listed up front, category by category — useful as a stand-alone QA report even before anything is placed, the same "count what's wrong before touching it" framing `Round Element Dist.pushbutton` and the already-logged Warning Auditor idea both open with.
4. **Propose a placement per element** — anchor point reuses `AutoDIM`'s own `bbox_center()`/`element_frame()` (or the promoted `anongee_toolkit.revit.dimensioning` module if the already-logged Dimension Sweep idea has landed by then) to find a clear point just outside the element's own footprint, on the side with the most open space; if that box would overlap another tag, a dimension's bounding box, or a grid/level bubble from step 2, the tag is offset further out and given a leader (`Tag.HasLeader = True`, elbow placed at the first offset that clears everything) rather than left overlapping — the genuinely new piece nothing in the repo does today: spatial collision-testing between annotation elements.
5. **Preview checklist** (`anongee_toolkit.ui.hostwnd`/`checklist`/`progressbar`, the same shell `ConvertFloor.pushbutton` and `AutoDIM`'s proposed siblings already run on) — one row per element: category, Mark/Type value the tag will display, placement (**inline** / **leadered — cleared N conflicts**), and a **Locate** action per row (the same isolate-and-zoom call the already-logged Link Position Guard and Lap & Starter-Bar Continuity Auditor ideas both cite reusing); rows tick/untick before anything is placed.
6. **Place** — one `Transaction`; `IndependentTag.Create()` per ticked row using the project's own default tag family per category (falling back to prompting for one, per category, the first time a category has none loaded); one row's placement failure is caught and reported, not fatal to the rest of the sweep.
7. **Report** — placed / already-tagged-skipped / leadered-to-avoid-conflict / blocked (no tag family loaded for this category) counts, exportable via the same `openpyxl` path `Export Schedule.pushbutton`/`BBS Generator.pushbutton` already use — a per-sheet "tagging complete, N leadered" record for QA sign-off.

**Implementation:**
- Reuses `AutoDIM.pushbutton`'s `element_frame`, `planar_faces`, `extreme_faces`, `bbox_center` (`script.py:70-150`) for the anchor-point read — directly, or via the shared `anongee_toolkit.revit.dimensioning` module if the already-logged Dimension Sweep idea has already promoted them, making this the third caller rather than a second copy of the same face-reading math.
- New `anongee_toolkit.revit.tagging` module — the actual new logic, since no tag-placement or annotation-collision code exists anywhere in the repo today (confirmed by the `IndependentTag`/`TagOrientation` grep above): `existing_annotations(view)` collecting tag/dimension/datum-bubble bounding boxes, `find_clear_point(anchor, avoid_boxes, tag_size)` doing a simple ring-search outward from the anchor until a candidate box clears every entry in `avoid_boxes`, and `place_tag(doc, view, element, point, leadered)` wrapping `IndependentTag.Create`.
- Tag bounding-box estimate for collision testing uses `IndependentTag.get_BoundingBox(view)` on a first, un-checked placement (create, measure, then either keep it or delete and retry leadered) rather than trying to predict text-box size from font metrics ahead of time — matches the "create, verify, roll back if wrong" posture `Convert Slab.pushbutton`'s own pre-flight-then-commit pattern already uses at the transaction level, just applied per-tag instead of per-run.
- Default tag family per category resolved the same way `RC Automation.pushbutton`/`BBS Generator.pushbutton` already resolve their own shared-parameter/family dependencies idempotently on first run — checked, reused if already loaded, prompted for once per category otherwise.
- `tag_sweep_config.json` alongside `script.py` (which categories default-ticked, tag family per category, leader elbow style, minimum clearance margin in mm), mirroring `slab_convert_config.json`'s override-by-config shape every other configurable tool in the repo already uses.
- Sits in `Essential.panel/Action.stack`, next to `AutoDIM.pushbutton`, with its own `bundle.yaml`/`README.md`/`CHANGELOG.md` following the layout the root README documents for versioned tools.

**Future update:**
- Consume Grid Mark Assistant's proposed output directly once that tool ships: prefer its resolved grid-intersection point as the tag's untagged anchor instead of the plain bbox center, closing the exact loop that idea's own future-update list names.
- Multi-view batch mode, matching the already-logged Dimension Sweep idea's own proposed "one pass per level" future item, so a modeler tags an entire building's structural plans in one sitting instead of view by view.
- Re-run/diff mode: a later sweep only tags elements added since the last run and leaves already-placed, manually-adjusted tags untouched — the same "re-run and diff" shape already logged as a future item for Type Matrix Generator, the Sleeve Coordinator and Grid Mark Assistant.
- Extend collision-avoidance to detail components and text notes once tag/dimension/datum coverage is proven, for sheets that carry heavier manual annotation on top of the model-driven tags.

## 2026-09-17 10:45 UTC

**Idea name:** Analytical-Physical Alignment Auditor — whole-model check and batch fix for structural elements whose analytical model has drifted from, or was never linked to, the physical element it represents

**Potential:**
Every structural physical element (column, beam, floor, wall, foundation) that carries `Analytical Model = Yes` is supposed to have a matching analytical line/plane feeding load paths into Robot Structural Analysis, ETABS, SAFE, STAAD or an IFC structural-analysis handoff — but Revit only lets a modeler inspect and fix that association **one element at a time**, via the Analytical Model panel's "Auto Detect"/manual-offset toggle on a single selected element, with no whole-model report of what's actually out of sync. In practice this drifts silently: a physical element moved without regenerating its analytical counterpart, an analytical element whose manual alignment override was never reset after a design change, or — most concretely, already proven inside this very repo — a physical element that gets deleted and recreated (exactly what `Convert Slab.pushbutton` does on every floor↔foundation conversion) leaving its analytical element orphaned. `ConvertFloor.pushbutton/script.py:609-610` explicitly detects `"Analytical" in d.GetType().Name` among an element's dependents, and `script.py:1231-1232` surfaces `"analytical association will be lost"` as a pre-flight warning — but that check exists only as a side-effect warning inside one conversion tool, fires only on elements that tool happens to touch, and does nothing to find or fix analytical debris anywhere else in the model. A grep across every idea already logged here and every script in `AnonGee.tab` turns up exactly one other hit for "analytical" at all (`ConvertFloor.pushbutton/script.py`, the citation above) — nothing in this toolkit, and nothing free on the market, audits physical↔analytical consistency in one pass. The only tools that touch this space are whole-discipline paid QA suites (Ideate Sidekick's Model Checker, Autodesk Model Coordination) that don't specifically target analytical/physical association, or the manual one-element-at-a-time native workflow structural modelers already fall back to when a Robot/ETABS export throws an offset-support error and nobody can tell which of 200 columns caused it. Catching this before export — rather than after a structural engineer reports an offset support three days later — is exactly the kind of silent, compounding, model-wide risk this toolkit already treats as worth automating (the same posture behind the already-logged Warning Auditor and Link Position Guard ideas), just never yet pointed at the analytical model itself.

**Description:**
A new pushbutton, `Analytical Alignment Auditor.pushbutton`, added to `Essential.panel/StrucBIM.stack/` alongside `Convert Slab.pushbutton`, `Draw Floor.pushbutton` and `Round Element Dist.pushbutton` — the stack already reserved for structural geometry-integrity tools.

1. **Scope** — pre-selection, active view, or whole model, the same three-way picker `Convert Slab.pushbutton` already offers.
2. **Audit pass**, per structural-capable element (columns, framing, floors, walls with a structural usage, foundations):
   - Read `STRUCTURAL_ANALYTICAL_MODEL` — is analytical generation enabled for this instance.
   - If enabled, look up its associated analytical element (built once per run as a whole-model reverse map, see Implementation). No association found → flagged **orphaned physical** (analytical generation is on, but nothing was ever built or the link was lost).
   - If an association exists, compare the analytical geometry (curve for line-based members, mid-plane for area-based panels) against the physical element's own centerline/mid-plane; anything beyond a configurable tolerance (default 5 mm) is flagged **drifted**, with the offset distance reported.
   - Separately, every analytical element **not** referenced by any physical element in the reverse map is flagged **orphaned analytical** — leftover debris from a deleted-and-recreated physical element, the exact risk `ConvertFloor.pushbutton` already warns about but never cleans up model-wide.
3. **Report table** — category, mark, level, status (OK / drifted _X_ mm / orphaned physical / orphaned analytical), sortable and filterable, exportable to Excel through the same `openpyxl` path `Export Schedule.pushbutton` / `BBS Generator.pushbutton` already use.
4. **Batch fix**, opt-in per ticked row only: re-enable + regenerate analytical model, reset a drifted element back to auto-detected alignment, or delete a confirmed orphaned analytical element — never applied to an unticked row, one `TransactionGroup` with one `Transaction` per element so a single failure doesn't roll back the batch.
5. **Pre-export checklist mode** — the same audit, framed as a go/no-go summary ("14 elements drifted >5 mm, 3 orphaned analytical elements") meant to be run immediately before a Robot/ETABS/SAFE analytical export or an IFC structural handoff, so an offset-support error is caught inside Revit instead of surfacing downstream in the analysis software.

**Implementation:**
- New `anongee_toolkit/structural/analytical.py` module, sibling to `structural/columns.py` and `structural/framing.py` — the first module in the repo to touch `Autodesk.Revit.DB.Structure.AnalyticalMember` / `AnalyticalPanel` / `AnalyticalToPhysicalAssociationManager` at all; today's only "analytical" awareness in the whole codebase is `ConvertFloor.pushbutton/script.py:579,609-610`'s dependent-element detection, which never touches the analytical geometry API itself.
- Reverse association map: `AnalyticalToPhysicalAssociationManager.GetAnalyticalToPhysicalAssociationManager(doc)`, then `FilteredElementCollector(doc).OfClass(AnalyticalMember)` + `.OfClass(AnalyticalPanel)`, calling `manager.GetAssociatedElementId(analytical_id)` per element to build a `physical_id -> analytical_id` map once per run — there is no built-in physical→analytical lookup, only the reverse, so both the drift check and the orphan check reuse this one map.
- Enabled flag: `BuiltInParameter.STRUCTURAL_ANALYTICAL_MODEL`, the exact parameter `ConvertFloor.pushbutton/script.py:849` already carries in its `SKIP_BIPS` set (excluded there because copying it verbatim across a floor→foundation recreate is unsafe, not because it can't be read) — this tool is the first to read it for its own sake rather than skip it during a copy.
- Drift geometry: line-based elements reuse the physical centerline the way `structural/framing.py:256`'s `_midpoint` and `structural/columns.py`'s placement helpers already derive one from `LocationCurve`/`LocationPoint`, compared against `AnalyticalMember.GetCurve()`; area-based elements compare against the physical `Solid`'s mid-plane using the same `Solid` iteration `get_solid_volume_m3` (`revit/geometry.py:10-37`) already opens, extended to a plane fit instead of a volume sum.
- Orphaned-analytical detection reuses the same "collect everything, then set-difference against what's actually referenced" shape the already-logged Bulk Purge idea (2026-09-15) uses for View Templates/Filters/Materials — a second consumer of that pattern, here against the association map instead of `GetFilters()`/`CompoundStructure` layers.
- Fix actions (`STRUCTURAL_ANALYTICAL_MODEL` write-back, alignment reset, orphan `doc.Delete()`) go through the same one-`TransactionGroup`/one-`Transaction`-per-element pattern `Convert Slab.pushbutton` already establishes.
- UI: the same checklist-with-per-row-status shell (`anongee_toolkit.ui.hostwnd` / `progressbar` / `checklist` / `theme`) every other multi-step tool in the repo runs on, status-tinted (OK / drifted / orphaned) instead of pass/fail.
- Sits in `Essential.panel/StrucBIM.stack/`, following the same `bundle.yaml` / `README.md` / `CHANGELOG.md` layout the root README documents for versioned tools.

**Future update:**
- Tolerance-by-category config (columns held tighter than walls) instead of one flat default, same per-category config-file shape `ConvertFloor.pushbutton`'s `slab_convert_config.json` already uses.
- Extend orphan detection to `AnalyticalLink`/boundary-condition elements once member/panel coverage is proven, so a support that silently detached from its host is caught in the same pass.
- Hook the pre-export checklist into `RC Automation.pushbutton`'s own pre-flight, so a reconciliation run refuses to proceed past a model with unresolved analytical drift.
- A "since last run" diff mode — the same shape the already-logged Sheet Issue Kit idea (2026-09-15) proposes for re-issuance — so a re-audit after a design change reports only what's newly drifted, not the whole model again.

## 2026-09-17 05:40 UTC

**Idea name:** Rebar Cage Copier — duplicate one modeler-built rebar set onto many matching columns, beams, footings or walls, auto-fitted to each target's own size

**Potential:**
Every rebar tool already in this repo works on rebar that already exists in the model — `Copy Rebar Visibility.pushbutton` copies *view visibility flags* for rebar between views, `Rebar Cover Auditor` (logged 2026-09-16) checks the *cover* of placed bars, `Bar Cutting Optimizer` (logged 2026-09-16) nests an *already-generated* bar list, and `RC Automation` (`AnonGee.tab/Dev.panel/col2.stack/RC Automation.pushbutton`) is a multi-quarter effort to *generate* rebar layouts from code standards, with its own todo-list.md admitting footings alone "have taken seven versions and their constraints are still unconfirmed" and beams/columns/walls (*Bridge · FEAT-17*) filed as `pending`, "quarters, not weeks." None of them address the single most repetitive action a structural modeler does today: **hand-building the same rebar cage over and over** — a typical floor might carry 40 identical `300x450` columns, and once the first one is correctly detailed (main bars, ties/stirrups, spacing, hooks, cover), the other 39 are built by manually repeating the same `Rebar.CreateFromRebarShape` clicks, or by copy-pasting the rebar and then nudging every bar length and tie count by hand wherever a target column's height, width or count of faces differs even slightly. That gap — no way to say "make this element's cage look like that one, already-verified element's cage, adjusted for its own dimensions" — is exactly what this button closes, and it needs none of RC Automation's hard, still-unsolved problem (generating a layout from a code standard) because the layout is never invented; it is only replicated from a template the modeler already trusts. On the market, "rebar detailing" plugins that exist (iRevit RCDF, Naviate Structure's rebar tools, BIM ONE's structural extensions) are paid, seat-licensed add-ins bundled with far more than this one action, and even they mostly generate from rules rather than clone-and-refit an existing, human-approved cage — nothing free does the specific "copy this proven cage, refit to a different host size" job.

**Description:**
A new pushbutton, `Copy Rebar Cage.pushbutton`, added to the existing `Rebar.pulldown` (`AnonGee.tab/Essential.panel/Bulk Tool.stack/Rebar.pulldown/`) next to `Copy Rebar Visibility.pushbutton` and `Obscured Rebar.pushbutton`.

1. **Pick the source** — select one host element (column, beam, footing or wall) that already carries a correct rebar set; the tool reads every `Rebar` element hosted on it via `RebarHostData.GetRebarHostData(host).GetRebarsInHost()`, capturing per bar: its `RebarShape`, bar diameter (`RebarBarType`), the shape-driving dimensions, spacing rule (fixed number / max spacing / min spacing), cover references, and hook types at each end.
2. **Pick the targets** — select any number of other elements from the same category, pre-filtered to the same family/type as the source (mismatched categories are listed as skipped, not silently attempted).
3. **Fit strategy per target** — for each target, the source cage's *layout rule* is replayed rather than its raw geometry: a "3 legs @ 150 c/c" tie spacing is regenerated to fit the target's actual length, main-bar count is kept fixed while bar length stretches or shrinks to the target's own face-to-face distance, and cover distances re-resolve against the target's own faces (reusing the cover-to-face resolution `Rebar Cover Auditor` already established as a whole-model, code-checked read). A live preview table shows, per target, what will change (bar count, computed length, any dimension too small to fit the source's spacing rule, flagged rather than forced).
4. **Apply** — one `Transaction`, one rebar set created per target per source bar-set (existing rebar on a target is left untouched unless the modeler explicitly ticks "replace existing"), with a result report: cages copied / targets skipped (category mismatch, no room to fit) / bars flagged for manual check.

**Implementation:**
- Read side: `RebarHostData.GetRebarHostData(hostElement)` → `GetRebarsInHost()` → per `Rebar`, `GetShapeId()`, `GetBarTypeId()`, `GetHookTypeId(0/1)`, and the active `RebarLayoutRule`'s `GetDistributionType()`/`GetSpacing()`/`GetNumberOfBarPositions()` (Revit 2022+ `Rebar` API — matches the repo's stated baseline in `Convert Slab`'s README, "Revit 2022+, developed against 2025").
- Cover-to-face resolution reuses the same per-face lookup **Rebar Cover Auditor** (logged 2026-09-16, itself "generalized off RC Automation's own footing-only cover-write path") establishes as a shared, whole-model-safe helper — this button is a second, natural consumer of that helper rather than a third independent implementation, so it should land after (or alongside) that auditor's cover-resolution module ships.
- Write side: `Rebar.CreateFromRebarShape(doc, shape, barType, host, origin, xVec, yVec)` for the initial curve set, then `SetLayoutAsNumberWithSpacing`/`SetLayoutAsMaximumSpacing` (matching the source's captured distribution type) to replay the spacing rule against the target's own geometry — never a raw copy-and-stretch of the source's curves, which is what would break the moment a target's dimensions differ from the source's.
- UI: same `ui.xaml` shell shape as `Copy Rebar Visibility.pushbutton` (source picker + target checklist + Apply), with the added preview grid modeled on the checklist-with-per-row-status pattern `Convert Slab.pushbutton` already uses for its pre-flight list (blocked/flagged rows pre-unticked, same visual language).
- Sits as a third pushbutton inside the existing `Rebar.pulldown/bundle.yaml` grouping — no new panel, stack or pulldown needed.

**Future update:**
- Multi-source "cage families" — save a verified cage as a named template (e.g. "300x450 Column — Standard Tie") reusable across projects, not just copied from a live element in the current session.
- Cross-category source→target (e.g. copy a footing's bottom-mat pattern onto a mat-slab zone) once the single-category case is proven.
- Feed straight into `BBS Generator.pushbutton` once cages are copied, so the newly-created bars appear in the next bar-bending-schedule run without a separate re-scan step.
- A "diff mode" that compares an already-modeled target's cage against the source template and reports only what's different (missing ties, wrong spacing) — the copy tool's read side reused as an audit, the same way `Rebar Cover Auditor` was itself generalized off a narrower existing path.

## 2026-09-17 00:39 UTC

**Idea name:** Formwork & Shuttering Takeoff — per-face contact-area quantity survey for columns, beams, walls, slabs and footings, split into shutter-needed vs no-shutter-needed surfaces straight from live model geometry

**Potential:**
Every RC element that gets poured needs formwork, and every formwork order needs the same number an estimator or site engineer currently has no direct way to get out of the model: total *contact area* of shuttering ply/panel, not volume and not Revit's own whole-face area parameter. Revit computes `HOST_AREA_COMPUTED` per host, but it is a single undifferentiated number per element (a floor's top face, a wall's one reported face) that mixes shutter-needed surfaces with surfaces that never touch formwork at all — a slab-on-grade's underside (poured on blinding, not shuttered), a footing's base (poured against earth or PCC), a column's top (open for casting) — and it doesn't exist at all for columns, beams or footings, only floors and walls. Confirming this by reading the one place in the repo that actually touches `HOST_AREA_COMPUTED` — `ConvertFloor.pushbutton/script.py:847` explicitly adds it to `SKIP_BIPS` so it's never even carried over as a parameter during a floor↔foundation conversion, and the only other read of it, `measured()` at `script.py:932-939`, uses it purely as a single before/after regression number to confirm a converted slab's top face didn't shrink — never split by face orientation, never summed across a level, never turned into a takeoff. Today, getting a shuttering quantity means exporting a schedule of element dimensions and calculating side/soffit area by hand per element type (perimeter × height for columns, width × 2 + soffit width for beams, length × height for walls minus openings), a spreadsheet exercise redone from scratch on every design revision — exactly the kind of repetitive, geometry-derived number this toolkit already automates for reinforcement (BBS Generator's `BarRecord.cutting_length_mm`) and now, with the already-logged Bar Cutting Optimizer and Pour Break Manager ideas, for steel wastage and pour volume, but never yet for the concrete's own outer skin. The toolkit's only face-level geometry precedent is `AutoDIM.pushbutton/script.py:101-104`, which iterates `Solid.Faces` and filters to `DB.PlanarFace` purely to find dimension-string references — it reads face existence, never face area or orientation — and `anongee_toolkit.revit.geometry.get_solid_volume_m3` (`revit/geometry.py:10-37`) sums whole-`Solid.Volume` per element without ever touching an individual `Face`, confirming there is no face-classification helper anywhere in `anongee_toolkit` to build on; this would be the first tool in the repo to read `Face.Area` and `Face.FaceNormal` at all. On the market, formwork/shuttering quantity takeoff exists only inside paid whole-discipline 5D/QTO platforms (CostX, Assemble, Bexel Manager, Naviate's own QTO module) sold as a separate licensed seat on top of Revit, or done by exporting a model to a generic estimating package — nothing free reads a Revit structural model's own geometry and classifies its faces into shutter-needed vs not directly.

**Description:**
`Formwork Takeoff.pushbutton`, a new sibling to `Bay Fill.pushbutton`/`ConvertFloor.pushbutton` in `Essential.panel/StrucBIM.stack/` (concrete-quantity tooling belongs there, alongside the pushbuttons that already create and convert the elements being measured):

1. Collects columns, structural framing (beams), structural walls, floors/foundation slabs and structural foundations from a pre-selection, active view, or whole-model scope — the same three-way scope pattern `ConvertFloor.pushbutton` already offers its own element picker.
2. Per element, walks `Solid.Faces` (the same `get_Geometry`/`Solid` iteration `get_solid_volume_m3` already does in `revit/geometry.py:22-35`, extended to inspect each `Face` instead of only summing `Solid.Volume`) and classifies every planar face by its normal vector and the element's category: vertical faces on columns/walls/footings/beam sides count as **shutter-needed**; a beam's underside (near-horizontal, downward normal) counts as **shutter-needed** (soffit); a slab's underside counts as **shutter-needed** only when the slab isn't slab-on-grade (checked via the same `BuiltInParameter.FLOOR_PARAM_IS_STRUCTURAL`/base-level-at-lowest-level heuristic `ConvertFloor.pushbutton` already reads when resolving a floor's structural intent); a footing's base and a column's/wall's top count as **no-shutter** (earth-contact or open-to-pour) by default, both flags user-overridable per element or per type for the retaining-wall/backfilled-face edge cases a normal-and-category rule can't fully resolve alone.
3. Deducts face area at every element-to-element join the model already knows about (`Solid` boolean intersection area at a column-beam or beam-slab interface, the same monolithic-pour boundary a QS would hand-deduct) so a beam framing into a column isn't double-counted as needing formwork on the buried face, and flags — rather than guesses at — any join Revit reports as unresolved so nothing is silently over- or under-counted.
4. Live report: one row per element (category, mark, level, shutter area in m², no-shutter area in m², total contact area) rolling up to per-level and per-category subtotals, plus a running total panel area, sortable and filterable the same way `Export Schedule.pushbutton`'s own report table already works.
5. Read-only against the model — no `Transaction`, since nothing is written back unless the modeler explicitly opts into stamping a `Shutter Area` shared parameter per element (one optional, ticked-rows-only write, same "report-first, write only what's ticked" posture the already-logged Rebar Cover Auditor and Lap & Starter-Bar Continuity Auditor both use) — so it's safe to re-run after every design change with zero rollback risk.
6. Exports through the same `openpyxl` path `BBS Generator.pushbutton/output/excel_writer.py` and `Export Schedule.pushbutton` already use, one sheet per category plus a level-rollup summary sheet, ready to hand straight to a shuttering subcontractor or into a cost estimate.

**Implementation:**
- New `anongee_toolkit/revit/faces.py` (or extend `revit/geometry.py` directly, alongside `get_solid_volume_m3`): `classify_faces(element)` returning `(shutter_faces, no_shutter_faces)` with each face's resolved `Face.Area` in m² and its normal-derived orientation label — the genuinely new piece, since no face-level classification exists anywhere in the repo today (confirmed by the `PlanarFace`/`Faces`/`HOST_AREA_COMPUTED` grep above).
- Join deduction reuses the same `Solid` geometry already read for classification — a boolean-intersection area check against neighboring elements found via `bounding_boxes_overlap` (`revit/geometry.py:40-59`, already used elsewhere in the repo for coarse neighbor lookups before an exact geometry test) — no new spatial-index dependency.
- Slab-on-grade vs suspended-slab detection reuses `ConvertFloor.pushbutton`'s own existing structural-floor parameter reads rather than inventing a second heuristic.
- Optional `Shutter Area` shared-parameter write follows the same idempotent shared-parameter provisioning pattern `RC Automation.pushbutton`/`BBS Generator.pushbutton` already use for their own shared parameters.
- Excel export reuses `BBS Generator.pushbutton/output/excel_writer.py`'s existing colour palette and row-builder helpers verbatim (one new sheet-builder function, same as the already-logged Bar Cutting Optimizer idea proposes for its own report), rather than a second styling module.
- Per-category/per-type shutter-vs-no-shutter overrides stored the same config-file-alongside-`script.py` shape `ConvertFloor.pushbutton`'s `slab_convert_config.json` already uses.
- Sits in `Essential.panel/StrucBIM.stack/`, its own `bundle.yaml`/`README.md` following the layout the root README documents for versioned tools.

**Future update:**
- Feed shutter area straight into the already-logged Pour Break Manager's per-pour report, so a pour zone's Excel export carries formwork area alongside its concrete volume in one hand-off.
- Standard shutter-panel-size nesting (plywood sheet sizes, e.g. 1220×2440mm) against the classified face geometry, reusing the same 1D/2D bin-packing shape the already-logged Bar Cutting Optimizer proposes for stock-bar cutting, to minimize custom-cut panel wastage.
- Reusable-formwork-cycle counting: group faces by identical dimensions across repeating floors (typical in a multi-storey building) to report how many *unique* panel sets are actually needed versus total gross area, since the same shutter set is reused pour to pour.
- Extend classification to slab edge-forms and stepped-footing risers, the two shapes a pure face-normal rule under-handles today and would need a slightly richer per-edge check.

## 2026-09-16 15:40 UTC

**Idea name:** Link Position Guard — persisted transform baseline and drift alarm for every Revit link, so a moved or re-shared linked model is caught before a coordination pass runs against it

**Potential:**
A structural team's grids, columns and clash checks are only as trustworthy as the architectural/MEP links they're coordinated against, and Revit itself keeps no history of a link's own position: Manage Links shows path, status and pinned state, but nothing about whether the link sitting in front of you today is where it was when the last coordination pass was run against it. An architect resolving an unrelated warning, re-publishing from a different insertion point, or a consultant swapping in a "final" file with a shifted origin all move the link with zero on-screen signal beyond a plan that quietly looks a little off — and that's caught, if at all, by someone eyeballing a view days later, usually after real modelling work has already happened against the stale position. Nothing in this repo touches this today: grepping every script under `AnonGee.tab` and every module under `anongee_toolkit` for `RevitLinkInstance` and `GetTotalTransform` returns hits only in `cad2bim`'s CAD-import code (`anongee_toolkit/cad2bim/geom/transform.py:119-136`, `AnonGee.tab/Core.panel/cad2bim.pushbutton/script.py:428`), which reads an `ImportInstance`'s transform once, in the same session it was created, to place freshly-linked CAD geometry — never a `RevitLinkInstance`, and never persisted across sessions for later comparison. `ToggleLinked.pushbutton` (`ToggleLinked.pushbutton/script.py`, 69 lines total) is the repo's only other link-adjacent tool, and it only flips `OST_RvtLinks` category visibility on the active view (`SetCategoryHidden`, `script.py:62-66`) — it never reads a link's `GetTotalTransform()` at all. `anongee_toolkit.structural.coordinates` looks adjacent but solves a different axis entirely: `survey_offset_mm()`/`set_survey_offset_mm()` (`coordinates.py:22-125`) read and, deliberately rarely, write the **vertical** offset between the Project Base Point and Survey Point (a level's drawing elevation vs. its Revit elevation), and its own docstring is explicit that "it never moves either point" itself — it says nothing about a link's XY position or rotation, which is a separate number tracking a separate problem. Two already-logged ideas in this file name the general capability as wanted but neither builds it: Grid & Level Extent Sync's future-update list includes "flag drift on a later re-run" and View Graphics Sync's includes a "drift re-check mode," both still just bullet points. This would be the first tool in the repo to actually implement a persisted-baseline, diff-on-open pattern, aimed at the one case where an undetected drift is most expensive. On the market, link-position auditing exists only as a side effect of full coordination platforms — Revizto, Navisworks Clash Detective, BIM 360/ACC model issues — all paid, all seat-licensed, and all built to catch the *clash that results* from a moved link, not the move itself; a link that shifts into empty space with no resulting clash (common with a partial or early-phase MEP link) passes every one of them silently, where a direct transform check would still catch it.

**Description:**
`Link Position Guard.pushbutton`, new sibling to `ToggleLinked.pushbutton` in `Essential.panel/Modify.stack` — the repo's other `RevitLinkInstance`-touching tool, though this one reads a link's transform rather than its category visibility:

1. Enumerates every `RevitLinkInstance` in the model (`FilteredElementCollector(doc).OfClass(RevitLinkInstance)` — a new collector, nothing existing to reuse), reading each one's `UniqueId`, its `RevitLinkType`'s linked-file path via `ExternalFileReference.GetAbsolutePath()`, and its current `GetTotalTransform()`.
2. Resolves each transform to plain numbers: origin in millimetres via `anongee_toolkit.revit.units.ft_to_mm` — the same conversion helper `structural.coordinates.survey_offset_mm()` already imports (`coordinates.py:32`), so both modules share one source of truth for feet-to-mm — and a rotation angle in degrees from `BasisX`/`BasisY` projected onto the XY plane. An unloaded or unresolved link is caught and reported as **not loaded — position unknown** rather than raising, the same report-don't-crash posture every other audit tool in the repo already takes.
3. Diffs the current reading against a baseline kept in a per-project sidecar JSON (`link_position_guard_log.json`, alongside the model), following the same "small sidecar log keyed by persistent element identity, diffed on open" shape the already-logged Sheet Issue Kit idea proposes for its own `sheet_issue_kit_log.json` — this tool keys by link `UniqueId` instead of sheet `ElementId`, and would be the first one in the repo to actually build that pattern if it lands first.
4. Classifies every link: **baseline captured** (first time seen — recorded, nothing to compare yet), **unchanged**, **moved** (origin delta past a configurable mm tolerance, direction and distance shown), **rotated** (angle delta past a configurable degree tolerance), **file changed on disk** (the linked file's own last-write-time is newer than the baseline's capture time, independent of transform — a reload at the same position can still carry different content), **removed** (in the baseline, no longer in the model).
5. Live table, one row per link — name, status, position delta, rotation delta, file-modified flag — with a per-row **Locate** action (`UIDocument.ShowElements`/`Selection.SetElementIds`, the same call the already-logged Lap & Starter-Bar Continuity Auditor's own Locate action makes) so a flagged link is on screen in one click rather than hunted for in Manage Links.
6. Ticked rows get **accept as new baseline** — the tool's only write, and it writes to the sidecar JSON only, never to the Revit model or the link itself, so there is no `Transaction` anywhere in this tool at all, a stricter version of the "report-first, write only what's ticked" posture the already-logged Rebar Cover Auditor and Lap & Starter-Bar Continuity Auditor both use.
7. Report exportable through the same `openpyxl` path `Export Schedule.pushbutton`/`BBS Generator.pushbutton` already use, turning "the architectural link moved 340mm north sometime between Tuesday and today" into a dated, documented fact for a coordination meeting instead of something argued about from memory.

**Implementation:**
- New `anongee_toolkit.revit.link_transform` module — the genuinely new piece, since no `RevitLinkInstance`/`GetTotalTransform` read exists anywhere in the toolkit today outside `cad2bim`'s unrelated `ImportInstance` code (confirmed by the repo-wide grep above): one function enumerating links with their resolved origin/rotation/file-path/mtime, kept Revit-API-light so the diff/classify logic itself can sit in `tests/` the same way other structural modules in this repo already do.
- Unit conversion reuses `anongee_toolkit.revit.units.ft_to_mm` directly — the same helper `structural.coordinates.py:32` already imports — rather than a second feet-to-mm constant.
- Sidecar baseline read/write follows the shape the already-logged Sheet Issue Kit idea proposes for `sheet_issue_kit_log.json`; if that tool lands first, this one reuses its small JSON-log helper directly instead of writing a second one.
- File-staleness check is a plain `os.path.getmtime` stat against the resolved link path, degrading to **can't check — not locally reachable** rather than a false positive when the link is cloud-hosted or otherwise unreachable, matching the "report the limitation by name" posture `ConvertFloor.pushbutton`'s own blocked-case list already uses.
- UI is the same modeless window shell (`anongee_toolkit.ui.hostwnd`/`checklist`/`theme`) every other report tool in the repo already runs on; **Locate** reuses the same selection/zoom call the Lap & Starter-Bar Continuity Auditor's own Locate action already makes.
- `link_position_guard_config.json` alongside `script.py` (position tolerance in mm, rotation tolerance in degrees, whether file-mtime staleness is checked at all), mirroring `slab_convert_config.json`'s override-by-config shape every other configurable tool in the repo already uses.
- Sits in `Essential.panel/Modify.stack`, next to `ToggleLinked.pushbutton`, with its own `bundle.yaml`/`README.md` following the layout the root README documents for versioned tools.

**Future update:**
- Auto-check on document open via a pyRevit doc-opened event hook, surfacing a single non-blocking notification badge instead of requiring a manual click, once the manual version is proven in production.
- Extend the same baseline-diff engine to the host model's own grids (tying into the already-logged Grid & Level Extent Sync and Grid Mark Assistant ideas), so a detected link shift can also answer "does it still land on our grids," not just "did it move."
- Feed a moved-or-stale link straight into the already-logged Warning Auditor's report as one more category, so a single QA pass covers native Revit warnings and link-position drift together.
- Nested-link support: a link's own nested links compound their effective transform with the host link's and are flagged separately, since a nested link can drift independently of the link carrying it.

## 2026-09-16 10:39 UTC

**Idea name:** Bar Cutting Optimizer — stock-length nesting/cutting-pattern solver run directly on BBS Generator's own bar list, to cut steel wastage before the schedule ever leaves the model

**Potential:**
BBS Generator already produces an exact, per-bar cutting length for every placed rebar (`core/bar_record.py`'s `BarRecord.cutting_length_mm`, grouped by `diameter_mm`), but that list stops at "here is how long each bar needs to be" — it never answers the next question every site engineer and rebar estimator asks immediately after: "given 12m stock length TMT bars, how do I cut this list with the least wastage, and how many stock bars do I need to order?" Today that step happens entirely outside Revit and outside this toolkit: an estimator exports (or retypes) the BBS into a spreadsheet, sorts by diameter, and manually pairs/groups cutting lengths against 12m (or 11.7m/13m, per mill) stock bars — a classic 1D cutting-stock/bin-packing problem nobody in the office actually optimizes by hand, so leftover offcuts pile up unused and steel wastage on a typical RC job runs 3-8% over the theoretical BBS quantity, which is real money on every pour, not just a documentation nicety. Dedicated rebar-cutting-optimization software exists (RebarCAD's nesting module, Tekla Structures' rebar detailing add-ons, and standalone cutting-stock solvers sold to fabrication yards) but it is paid, sold as a separate seat, and none of it reads a Revit model directly — every one of them needs the same manual BBS export this toolkit could skip entirely. Nothing in `anongee_toolkit/structural/` or in BBS Generator itself does any stock-length nesting today (confirmed by grep — there is no `stock`, `nesting`, `waste`, or `cutting_pattern` anywhere in the repo), so this sits completely unaddressed even though the toolkit already owns every input the optimizer needs.

**Description:**
`Bar Cutting Optimizer.pushbutton`, a new sibling to `BBS Generator.pushbutton` in `Dev.panel/col1.stack/` (callable standalone, or as a "Optimize Cutting" button added straight into BBS Generator's own `ui/main_window.py` once the BBS run is on screen):

1. Reads the current BBS run's `BarRecord` list (the same list `output/excel_writer.py` already writes out) and groups every bar by `diameter_mm`, tallying `cutting_length_mm` × `no_of_bars` per group — no new Revit read needed, since BBS Generator has already done the model-side extraction.
2. Per diameter group, runs a 1D cutting-stock solve (first-fit-decreasing bin packing as the fast default, hooking a branch-and-bound/column-generation refinement pass behind an "optimize harder" toggle for large jobs) against a user-set stock length (12m default, editable, with a per-project saved list of the 2-3 stock lengths their supplier actually carries) and a configurable minimum-usable-offcut length (so a 150mm sliver isn't logged as "reusable" scrap).
3. Produces, per diameter: number of stock bars required, a cutting pattern per stock bar (which cut lengths come off which bar, in order), total wastage in kg and as a %, and a running comparison against "no optimization" (today's naive one-cut-per-length-per-stock-bar baseline) so the savings are visible in the same report.
4. Exports through the same `openpyxl` path BBS Generator's `output/excel_writer.py` already uses — one sheet per diameter, cutting pattern rows colour-coded the same way the BBS sheet already colour-codes new/changed/deleted bars — plus an optional site-floor cutting-list PDF via the existing `output/pdf_exporter.py` styling, so the bar-bender gets a physical cut sheet, not just a spreadsheet.
5. Read-only against the model — it touches nothing in Revit, only the already-extracted `BarRecord` data — so it carries none of BBS Generator's own transaction/rollback concerns and can be re-run instantly every time the BBS is revised.

**Implementation:**
- New `anongee_toolkit/structural/bar_optimizer.py` (or `core/cutting_optimizer.py` inside the pushbutton, mirroring where `bar_mark_manager.py`/`shape_resolver.py` already live) — the actual new logic, since nothing in the repo does bin-packing today.
- Solver takes a plain list of `(cutting_length_mm, no_of_bars)` tuples per diameter (built straight from `BarRecord.cutting_length_mm`/`.no_of_bars`/`.diameter_mm` — no schema change needed to `bar_record.py`) and returns a list of `(stock_bar_index, [cut_lengths])` patterns; first-fit-decreasing is ~30 lines of pure Python with zero new dependencies, keeping it IronPython 2 + CPython 3 compatible like the rest of `core/`.
- Diameter grouping and unit-weight-per-metre reuse `BarRecord.diameter_label`/`.unit_weight_kg_per_m` directly, so wastage-in-kg falls out of the same weight math BBS Generator already trusts for its own total-weight column — no second source of truth for bar weight.
- Excel/PDF output reuse `output/excel_writer.py`'s existing colour palette (`C_HEADER_BG`, `C_SUBTOTAL_BG`, etc.) and `_set_row`/`_fill`/`_font` helpers verbatim, adding one new sheet-builder function alongside the BBS sheet builder rather than a new styling module.
- Stock-length list and minimum-offcut threshold are the only new user settings; stored the same way BBS Generator already persists its own standard/param-mapping choices (`bundle.yaml`-adjacent project settings), so the optimizer remembers a project's supplier stock length without re-asking every run.
- Ships as its own pushbutton rather than a hidden BBS Generator step, since a run-independent "optimize this BBS export" action is also useful pointed at an already-exported BBS Excel file, not only a live Revit run.

**Future update:**
- Multi-diameter combined ordering report (round steel tonnage per diameter up to the supplier's minimum order increment), for direct hand-off to procurement.
- Feed optimized cutting patterns back as a `bar_detail`/note on each `BarRecord` (already logged Schedule Round-Trip's write-back path) so the physical cutting assignment is visible back in the BBS sheet, not just in a separate report.
- Cross-project stock-length reuse suggestion: flag offcuts from one pour's optimization long enough to satisfy a later pour's shorter bars, for site-wide (not just per-run) wastage reduction.
- Extend the same 1D solver to structural steel sections (already a distinct member list in `anongee_toolkit/structural/framing.py`) for jobs mixing RC and structural steel scope.

## 2026-09-16 05:39 UTC

**Idea name:** Pour Break Manager — sketch-driven concrete pour zoning, sequence numbering and per-pour volume/boundary export for slabs, walls and columns

**Potential:**
Every RC structure gets poured in stages, but nothing in the toolkit — or in Revit itself — models a "pour" as a first-class thing. Today a site/planning engineer works out pour breaks on a printed or PDF-marked-up plan, and a modeler translates that by hand: sketching filled-region pour boundaries for visualization, manually typing a Pour Sequence Number into a shared parameter per element (or per split piece, using native `Divide Parts` one element at a time with no batch tooling), and then building a volume-per-pour quantity take-off in a spreadsheet by manually summing element volumes against the marked-up drawing. On a multi-storey slab or a core-wall structure with 10-20 pour zones per level, that is hours of repetitive, error-prone bookkeeping every time the pour sequence is revised — and it's revised often, since pour breaks routinely move as the construction program changes. This toolkit already has the two things that make BBS Generator and RC Automation valuable — a codified, swappable rule-set pattern (`standards/IS_2502_2019.py`, `BS_8666_2020.py`, `ACI_315_2018.py`) and a proven Excel/PDF hand-off (`output/excel_writer.py`, `output/pdf_exporter.py`) — but there is no `parts`, `pour`, or per-element-volume helper anywhere in `anongee_toolkit/structural/` today (confirmed empty on both), so pour sequencing sits completely outside what the toolkit currently does, even though it's one of the most recurring coordination tasks on any concrete job. On the market this lives only inside full 4D-scheduling suites (Navisworks TimeLiner, Synchro, ALICE) that need a separate license and a separate model hand-off just to answer "how many m³ in Pour 3B, and which elements are in it" — nothing lightweight and Revit-native does it for free.

**Description:**
`Pour Break Manager.pushbutton`, a new sibling to `RC Automation.pushbutton` in `Dev.panel/col2.stack/` (or promoted straight to `Essential.panel/StrucBIM.stack/` alongside `Convert Slab.pushbutton`/`Draw Floor.pushbutton` once proven), turns a hand-drawn pour boundary into a tracked, quantified pour zone:

1. Modeler sketches (or picks an existing detail/filled-region) a closed pour-boundary curve loop on the active plan/section view, or picks a set of elements directly (a level's slab plus its supporting walls, say) to define one pour zone.
2. Elements whose geometry falls inside the boundary — or the picked set directly — are grouped into a **Pour**: a lightweight tracked entity (native `Parts` created via `PartUtils.CreateParts` where a single host needs splitting across two pours, e.g. one slab spanning a pour joint; whole-element assignment where it doesn't) stamped with a shared `Pour Sequence Number`, `Pour Zone`, `Pour Date (Planned)` and `Pour Date (Actual)` parameter set — created once, in `bundle.yaml`'s shared-parameter provisioning step, the same way other tools in this repo declare the shared parameters they depend on.
3. Live pour table: one row per pour zone — element count, category breakdown, and **volume**, read via each element's `HOST_VOLUME_COMPUTED` built-in parameter where present and via `Solid.Volume` on the element's own geometry otherwise (split parts use the part's own computed volume) — sortable by sequence number, with a same-zone-overlap check flagging any element assigned to two pours at once before it's allowed to save.
4. One `Transaction`/`TransactionGroup`, same per-zone-rollback posture `Convert Slab.pushbutton` already uses per-element, so a bad boundary pick on Pour 4 doesn't touch the seven pours already committed.
5. Result: a pour boundary filled-region per zone (for the coordination drawing), plus an Excel export — pour number, element list, category totals, volume, planned vs. actual date — through the same `openpyxl` write path `Export Schedule.pushbutton`/`BBS Generator.pushbutton` already use, ready to hand to the site team or import into a 4D tool as a bridge rather than a replacement.

**Implementation:**
- New `anongee_toolkit/structural/pours.py`: `assign_pour(doc, boundary_loop_or_ids, sequence_number, zone_name)` — the actual new logic, since nothing in the repo does boundary-containment element selection, `PartUtils` splitting, or volume aggregation today. Containment test reuses the same "is this element's location inside this closed loop" geometry `DrawFloor.pushbutton` already needs to find enclosed bays, generalized from bay-shaped loops to arbitrary pour boundaries.
- Volume read is new (`HOST_VOLUME_COMPUTED` lookup falling back to `Element.get_Geometry()` solid-volume summation) — there is no existing volume helper anywhere in `anongee_toolkit/structural/` to build on, confirmed by grep; this is the one piece with no internal precedent.
- Splitting reuses `PartUtils.AreElementsValidForCreateParts`/`CreateParts` directly against the Revit API — the repo has never called `PartUtils` before, so this is the second genuinely new API surface (alongside the volume read).
- Shared-parameter provisioning (`Pour Sequence Number`, `Pour Zone`, the two date parameters) follows the same pattern `RC Automation` and `BBS Generator` already use for their own shared/project parameters — declared once, checked-and-created idempotently on first run.
- Reporting reuses `output/excel_writer.py`'s structure from `BBS Generator.pushbutton` nearly verbatim (swap bar rows for pour rows), and the pour-boundary filled region draws through the same detail-curve creation `cad2bim`'s builders already do for imported CAD linework.
- A `standards/`-style folder isn't a code requirement here (pour sequencing is project/contractor convention, not a national code), so instead ships one `sequencing_rules.py` holding a couple of common numbering conventions (level-major `L2-P01`, zone-major `P01-L2`) a project can pick between — same swappable-module shape as the BBS standards folder, different content.
- Sits as its own pushbutton rather than folding into RC Automation, since pour zoning applies to slabs and walls RC Automation never touches, not just the footings/columns it places rebar for.

**Future update:**
- Colour-coded 3D view template per pour sequence (auto-generated view filters keyed off `Pour Sequence Number`), for a one-glance sequencing walkthrough — reusing the filter-creation path `cad2bim/builders/view_filters.py` already has for a different purpose.
- Link `Pour Date (Actual)` entry back from an Excel re-import (the same round-trip direction the already-logged Schedule Round-Trip idea builds generally), so site progress marked in a spreadsheet flows back into the model's own view filters automatically.
- Cross-check pour boundaries against the already-logged Rebar Cover Auditor's host list, flagging rebar that crosses a pour joint without a specified starter/lap detail.
- Export a pour-sequence animation (ordered image/DWF sequence) for a client-facing construction sequence pack, without needing a separate 4D tool for something this simple.

## 2026-09-16 00:39 UTC

**Idea name:** Rebar Cover Auditor — whole-model, code-minimum cover check against the actual constrained bar-to-face distance, generalized off RC Automation's own footing-only cover-write path

**Potential:**
Concrete cover is the single reinforcement dimension most likely to be wrong in a hand-detailed or imported model and least likely to be caught before it reaches a drawing: Revit shows a bar's *shape* in every view, but nothing in its own UI reports the actual distance from a bar to the nearest formwork face, and "Edit Constraints → Constrain to Cover" only tells you whether a handle *is* tied to cover, one bar and one handle at a time — there is no whole-model report of what every bar's cover actually resolves to versus what the design intends. On a column-heavy or footing-heavy structural model that's dozens to hundreds of individual bars whose cover only becomes visible when a site engineer complains about a bar showing through formwork or a code-compliance reviewer flags it late, at which point fixing it means re-opening constraints bar by bar. This repo already built the two hard pieces this needs — `anongee_toolkit/structural/rebar_constraints.py`'s `distance_to_cover_mm()`/`describe()` (reads a shape-driven bar's actual signed gap to its constrained cover face, built for a one-off diagnostic dump) and `anongee_toolkit/structural/rebar_factory.py`'s `describe_cover()`/`set_host_cover()` (reads/writes a *host's* declared cover parameters, built only for footings RC Automation itself places) — but both are internal, single-purpose helpers wired only into RC Automation's own footing-placement flow (`RC Automation.pushbutton/script.py:311-327`); neither has ever been exposed as a standalone, whole-model, any-category audit a modeler can run over rebar that RC Automation didn't place — hand-drawn column ties, beam stirrups, slab mesh, or anything inherited from a linked/imported model. On the market, rebar cover checking exists only inside expensive whole-discipline QA suites (Naviate Structure's reinforcement checks, CSI-class rebar detailing add-ins) sold per seat as part of a much larger bundle; nothing free reports a bar's actual geometric cover against a code-minimum table at all.

**Description:**
`Rebar Cover Auditor.pushbutton`, sibling to `Copy Rebar Visibility.pushbutton` / `Obscured Rebar.pushbutton` in `Essential.panel/Bulk Tool.stack/Rebar.pulldown/`, following the same report-first, approve-each-fix posture `Round Element Dist.pushbutton` already established for a different dimension (element-to-datum distance) on the same panel:

1. Collects rebar — pre-selection if there is one, otherwise every `Rebar`/`RebarInSystem` element in the model, grouped by host category (Column, Beam, Foundation, Floor, Wall).
2. For each bar, resolves two numbers: **actual cover** — `distance_to_cover_mm()` per shape-driven handle already tied to a cover face (skipping handles tied to a face, not cover, and free-form bars, both named explicitly as "not checkable this way" rather than silently reported as compliant) — and **required cover** — the host's own declared `CLEAR_COVER_TOP/BOTTOM/OTHER` parameter when set, else a code-minimum looked up by exposure condition and bar diameter from a new minimum-cover table (IS 456:2000 Table 16, ACI 318 Table 20.6.1.3.1, or BS 8500, selected the same way BBS Generator already lets a project pick IS 2502 / BS 8666 / ACI 315).
3. Live table, one row per bar (grouped and collapsible by host element): host, face, actual cover, required cover, delta, status (OK / under / over / unconstrained / no-parameter). Sorted worst-first, same "report every deviation first" framing `Round Element Dist` already uses.
4. Ticked rows only get touched: for a bar whose handle is already constrained to the right face but sitting at the wrong distance, offers to re-run `constrain_to_cover()` against the correct cover type (creating/reusing one via `ensure_cover_type()` exactly as `set_host_cover()` already does for footings); for a bar with no host cover parameter set at all, offers to write it via `set_host_cover()` first. Bars this can't safely auto-fix (free-form geometry, no constraint manager on this Revit build, a handle tied to a face rather than cover) are listed with the reason and left for manual correction — never guessed at.
5. One `Transaction`, undo in one step; result report + Excel export (old/new cover per bar) for a documented QA record, reusing the `openpyxl` write-back path already proven in `Export Schedule.pushbutton` / `BBS Generator.pushbutton`.

**Implementation:**
- Read path is almost entirely existing code, just newly exposed at whole-model scope instead of one-bar-at-a-time: `anongee_toolkit/structural/rebar_constraints.py`'s `manager_for()`, `is_shape_driven()`, `handles()`, `distance_to_cover_mm()` and `target_face()` (today only reachable through `describe()`'s single-rebar diagnostic string) become the per-bar data source; `anongee_toolkit/structural/rebar_factory.py`'s `describe_cover()` supplies which cover parameter each host actually exposes.
- Fix path reuses `rebar_factory.ensure_cover_type()` (cache-keyed by face+value, exactly as documented against the three-duplicate-type bug it already fixed for RC Automation) and `rebar_constraints.constrain_to_cover()` for the "re-tie to correct cover" action, and `rebar_factory.set_host_cover()` for the "host has no cover parameter yet" action — all three called today only from inside RC Automation's footing-placement transaction, none from a UI a modeler drives directly.
- New piece this needs that nothing in the repo has today: a minimum-cover-by-exposure table, `standards/cover_minimums.py` (one per code, mirroring the `standards/IS_2502_2019.py` / `BS_8666_2020.py` / `ACI_315_2018.py` layout BBS Generator already uses for bending tables) keyed by exposure class and bar diameter, used only as the fallback when a host has no explicit cover parameter set.
- UI: a grouped, sortable results table matching `Round Element Dist`'s worst-first / tick-to-fix / skip-with-reason shell, reusing the shared WPF `Controls.xaml`/`Panels.xaml` theme every other tool already draws from.
- Sits in `Essential.panel/Bulk Tool.stack/Rebar.pulldown/`, next to `Obscured Rebar.pushbutton` and `Copy Rebar Visibility.pushbutton`, same `bundle.yaml`/`README.md`/`CHANGELOG.md` layout the root README documents for versioned tools.

**Future update:**
- Extend the exposure-condition lookup to read a project parameter (e.g. "Exposure Class") per host instead of one project-wide setting, for a model mixing footings (severe exposure) and internal columns (mild exposure).
- Add clear bar-to-bar spacing (not just cover-to-face) as a second check column, using the same per-handle geometry once cover is proven in production.
- Feed the "no cover parameter set" list into `Parameter Combination`/the filter-parameter tools as a one-click batch-set action across every host sharing a type, instead of one host at a time.
- A combined structural QA dashboard alongside the already-logged Lap & Starter-Bar Continuity Auditor and Column Stack Auditor, one pass over the whole rebar model with one summary report.

## 2026-09-15 15:40 UTC

**Idea name:** Sheet Issue Kit — one-click PDF + DWG + NWC export of a rule-picked sheet set, token-named, with a since-last-run diff so a re-issue only touches what actually changed

**Potential:**
Every drawing issue — a weekly coordination set, a milestone submission, a tender package — repeats the same non-modelling admin for every sheet in scope: open Revit's PDF export dialog, hand-pick or reuse a saved sheet set, set naming, export; reopen the completely separate DWG export dialog, re-pick the same sheets (its own naming options don't match the PDF dialog's), export; if Navisworks coordination is also owed, a third dialog, a third naming pass — three independent settings screens, repeated in full for every issue, even when only three of forty sheets actually changed since last week. Nothing in this repo touches this at all: grepping every script under `AnonGee.tab` and every module under `anongee_toolkit` for `PDFExportOptions`, `DWGExportOptions`, `ExportOptions`, `ViewSheetSet`, `PrintRange`, `IPrintSetup`/`GetPrintSetup` and `ExportImage` returns zero Revit API hits anywhere in the codebase (the `pythonwin`/`ezdxf` vendored-library hits for `printRange`/`.print()` are unrelated MFC/CLI code, not Revit). The one place "PDF" appears at all, `Export Schedule.pushbutton`'s `ChkSavePdf` checkbox (`script.py:309,421`) feeding `_excel_polish(xlsx_path, want_pdf)` (`script.py:669-732`), is a different job entirely: it drives **Excel COM** (`xlTypePDF`, `script.py:728`) to print the *just-exported .xlsx workbook* to PDF, degrading gracefully when Excel isn't installed (`script.py:673`, "degrades gracefully without Excel") — it never calls `Document.Export`, never touches a `View`/`ViewSheet`, and has nothing to do with issuing drawing sheets. The view-enumeration helper that exists, `anongee_toolkit.revit.views.get_eligible_views()`/`ALLOWED_VIEW_TYPES` (`revit/views.py:9-17,35-56`), explicitly excludes `ViewSheet` from its allowed set — built for element-visibility tools, not sheet issuance — so it cannot be reused here as-is. The only code that reads a sheet's own `SheetNumber`/`Name` at all is `anongee_toolkit.structural.inventory._views()` (`structural/inventory.py:323-341`), and only as one read-only field in `Extract Template.pushbutton`'s audit report — never as a live, selectable list, and never combined with a titleblock read for per-sheet paper size. Revit's own `Revision`/issue-date concept (`ViewSheet.GetCurrentRevision`, `SHEET_ISSUE_DATE`) is likewise never read anywhere in the repo; the only "revision" in the codebase is `BBS Generator.pushbutton`'s own unrelated Excel-sidecar diff (`chk_revision`/`tb_revision`, `ui/main_window.py:193-200,423-434`), which tracks bar-list changes between two spreadsheet snapshots, not a sheet's Revit revision. Revit's native PDF/DWG exporters can already batch off a manually pre-built, saved `ViewSheetSet` element, but that native path still forces three separate dialogs (PDF, DWG, and — if owed — a fourth-party Navisworks exporter) with three independent, non-token, non-parameter-aware naming schemes reconfigured by hand on every single issue, and it has no concept of "only the sheets that changed since the set was last issued" — every run re-exports the whole set regardless of what actually moved. On the market, wrapping the native exporter in a single multi-format pass with templated file names is exactly the headline feature CTC's BIM Tools, Ideate Sheets/BIMLink, and IMAGINiT Clarity sell as one line item of a much larger paid, per-seat suite; none of them — free or paid — skip re-exporting an unchanged sheet on a re-issue.

**Description:**
`Sheet Issue Kit.pushbutton` (new tool in `Essential.panel/Action.stack`, sibling to `Export Schedule.pushbutton` and forking that tool's `ExportScheduleDialog` shell — search box, checklist, badge triplet — for sheets instead of schedules) runs in one window:

1. **Sheet list** — every `ViewSheet` in the model (`FilteredElementCollector(doc).OfClass(ViewSheet)`, the same collector `structural.inventory._views()` already proves at `inventory.py:337`, promoted from a read-only audit field to a live, checkable list), shown with the same search-and-checkbox `ListBoxItem` pattern `ExportScheduleDialog._populate`/`_on_search_changed` already build (`Export Schedule.pushbutton/script.py:329-359`), all ticked by default.
2. **Filter to scope, not just tick by hand** — an optional rule row (parameter + value: discipline, a sheet-list "Revision" text parameter, a Yes/No "Issue" checkbox parameter, or a saved Revit `ViewSheetSet` name if one already exists) that pre-ticks the matching subset via `anongee_toolkit.revit.parameters.get_parameter_value` (`revit/parameters.py:36-82`) — the same read function the already-logged Schedule Round-Trip and Parameter Migrator ideas both call, this becomes a further caller — so a "just the structural set" or "just what's marked Issue = Yes" re-issue doesn't require re-hand-picking sheets from a 200-sheet list every week.
3. **Since-last-run diff** — the genuinely new piece nothing in the repo or, as far as this codebase's own market research shows, any competing add-in does: a small per-project sidecar JSON (`sheet_issue_kit_log.json`, alongside the model, one entry per sheet keyed by `ElementId` storing the sheet's content-hash — every placed view's `ElementId` + last-changed `Element.UniqueId`/`Document.GetElement(id).VersionNumber` pairing already exposed by the Revit API — and the export timestamp) is diffed against the current model on open; each ticked sheet shows **unchanged since last issue** or **changed — will export**, with a one-click "export everything anyway" override, so a Friday re-issue after three sheets changed doesn't silently regenerate all forty.
4. **Formats** — independently toggleable PDF / DWG / NWC checkboxes; PDF via `Document.Export(folder, name, ViewSet, PDFExportOptions)`, DWG via `Document.Export(folder, name, viewIds, DWGExportOptions)`, NWC via the Navisworks exporter's `NavisworksExportOptions` when that add-in is installed — detected once at startup and the NWC checkbox disabled with an inline reason rather than failing mid-run if it isn't, the same "degrade, don't crash" posture `Export Schedule.pushbutton`'s own Excel-COM absence handling already proves (`script.py:673`).
5. **File naming** — a token template (`{SheetNumber}_{SheetName}_{Revision}_{Date}`) applied per sheet per format, reusing the token formatter the already-logged Universal Renamer idea proposes if that tool has landed by the time this one is built, otherwise a small standalone `re`-based formatter promoted into the shared one the moment Universal Renamer exists — the same fallback posture the already-logged Sheet Set Builder idea commits to for its own naming step.
6. **Preview checklist** (`anongee_toolkit.ui.checklist.CheckList`, the shell every other batch tool in the repo already runs on) — one row per (sheet, format): resolved file name, size/scale read off the sheet's own titleblock via `structural.inventory._views()`'s existing title-block enumeration (`inventory.py:343-...`), and status (**will export** / **unchanged — skip** / **blocked — NWC exporter not installed**).
7. **Export** — one pass through the ticked rows per format, each format's own `ExportOptions` object built once and reused across every sheet in that format rather than reconstructed per sheet; a single bad sheet (a corrupt titleblock, a locked output path) is caught and reported without aborting the rest of the run.
8. **Report** — exported / skipped-unchanged / blocked counts per format, the sidecar log updated for every sheet actually exported, and an `openpyxl` issue-record (reusing the styling constants `Export Schedule.pushbutton` already proves, `script.py:148-161`) naming exactly what went out in this issue — the artifact a modeler currently has to build by hand for a transmittal record.

**Implementation:**
- New `anongee_toolkit.revit.sheet_export` module — the genuinely new piece, since no `PDFExportOptions`/`DWGExportOptions`/`ViewSheetSet` call exists anywhere in the toolkit today (confirmed by the repo-wide grep above): one function per format wrapping `Document.Export`, and one `NavisworksExportOptions` probe that returns `None` rather than raising when the Navisworks exporter isn't installed.
- Sheet enumeration goes straight through the same `FilteredElementCollector(doc).OfClass(ViewSheet)` collector `structural.inventory._views()` (`inventory.py:337`) already proves, rather than a second hand-rolled collector; titleblock size/scale reuses that same function's title-block read (`inventory.py:343-...`).
- Parameter-rule filtering and diff-detection both go through `anongee_toolkit.revit.parameters.get_parameter_value` (`revit/parameters.py:36-82`), the same read function the already-logged Schedule Round-Trip and Parameter Migrator ideas call — no second parameter-read convention introduced.
- UI shell is a direct fork of `ExportScheduleDialog` (`Export Schedule.pushbutton/script.py:281-360`) — same `XamlReader.Load`, search box, badge triplet — with the schedule checklist swapped for a sheet checklist and one added filter-rule row.
- `sheet_issue_kit_config.json` alongside `script.py` (default naming template, default rule parameter name, which formats default-on, sidecar log path), mirroring `slab_convert_config.json`'s override-by-config shape every other configurable tool in the repo already uses.
- Excel issue-record export reuses the `openpyxl` path and brand constants already proven in `Export Schedule.pushbutton` (`script.py:148-161,455-...`).
- Sits in `Essential.panel/Action.stack/`, next to `Export Schedule.pushbutton`, its direct sibling and the tool it forks its dialog shell from.

**Future update:**
- Read a project's actual `ViewSheetSet` elements (if any already exist) as a fourth filter option alongside the parameter rule, so a firm that already maintains named sheet sets in-model doesn't have to re-express that grouping as a parameter rule.
- Feed the sidecar diff log into a "what changed since last issue" summary view, similar in spirit to the already-logged Warning Auditor and Dimension Sweep ideas' own re-run/diff modes, so a coordination meeting can open with "these six sheets moved since Tuesday" instead of a modeler reconstructing that by memory.
- Batched combined-PDF output (one file for the whole set, Revit's own "combine into one file" PDF option) as a togglable alternative to one-file-per-sheet, for submissions that want a single transmittal PDF rather than a folder of them.
- Tie the token naming template to the same shared formatter the already-logged Universal Renamer and Sheet Set Builder ideas both plan to converge on, so all three tools stop carrying independent standalone fallback formatters the moment one of them lands first.
