## 2026-09-22 05:43 UTC

**Idea name:** Stair Landing Completer — sweeps the model for dog-leg stair pairs `stairs.sync` placed without the mid-flight landing between them, and finishes each one with the exact `StairsLanding` API call the toolkit already proved elsewhere but never wired into the pipeline whose own docs call this gap out by name

**Potential:**
`stairs.sync` (`AnonGee.extension/lib/py3/anongee_toolkit/commands/stairs.py`, registered as `("stairs.sync", "stairs", "run")` in `commands/__init__.py:47`) is the RC Automation command that places real Revit `Stairs` from the `STAIR_PLACEMENT` sheet — Option A, chosen over a beam-and-slab "waist stair" the project owner rejected outright (`todo-list.md:1016`, `FEAT-42`, `testing`: *"building stairs with beam and slab is management nightmare"*). Its own module docstring admits, in the same breath as explaining the dog-leg grouping idiom, that the fix is incomplete: *"A dog-leg is two rows sharing a mark with a blank row after them, the same idiom that makes a continuous beam a group — and, for now, the same idiom that gets two separate straight flights, because **the landing between them is not placed yet**"* (`commands/stairs.py:10-13`). This is not a stray comment — there is a whole superseded-decision document, `docs/specs/2026-08-27-stairs-option-b.md`, that names it in a feature table (line 39: `| **Landing** | *Not built yet* | See below. This is the honest gap |`) and gives it its own section (`### 2. No mid-landing`, line 86), and `todo-list.md:1016` repeats it in the live `FEAT-42` row: *"No mid-landing yet"*, status still `testing`. Three independent documents agreeing the same feature is missing is as close to a confirmed, current gap as this repo gets. What none of them mention is that the fix already exists, shipped, in a different pipeline: `grep -rn "StairsLanding" AnonGee.extension --include=*.py` (excluding the vendored `tzdata` tree) returns exactly five lines, and every one is in `cad2bim/builders/stairs.py` or its own changelog comment — `:22` imports it, `:303` calls `StairsLanding.CreateAutomaticLanding(doc, first, second)`, `:388` calls `StairsLanding.CreateSketchedLanding(doc, stairs_id, loop, relative_elevation)`, and `cad2bim/__init__.py:831,837` mention it only in changelog prose. Zero hits in `structural/stairs.py` or `commands/stairs.py` — the sheet-driven pipeline that admits it needs exactly this call has never imported the class that makes it. The reason it isn't a one-line port: `structural/stairs.py:198-216`'s `create()` opens its own `StairsEditScope` per flight (`scope.Start(base_level.Id, top_level.Id)`, line 216), and `commands/stairs.py:49-50`'s `_parse()` docstring says plainly *"Groups flattened — a flight is a flight downstream"* — the dog-leg pairing `rc_automation/placement.py:399-408`'s `parse_stairs()` establishes upstream (*"a dog-leg is two rows sharing a mark... a dog-leg stair is two flights that make one stair"*) is deliberately thrown away before `_apply()` (`commands/stairs.py:65`) calls `stairs.create()` once per row at line 129, independently. Two rows sharing a mark become **two separate `Stairs` elements**, not two `StairsRun`s on one `Stairs` — and `StairsLanding.CreateAutomaticLanding` requires both runs on the *same* `Stairs`, exactly what cad2bim's single-scope, multi-`CreateStraightRun` approach (`cad2bim/builders/stairs.py:165-260`) sets up before it ever calls the landing API at line 303. So today, every dog-leg `stairs.sync` places ships with a gap where the landing should be, and the only fix is a modeler manually re-entering the stair's sketch and drawing the landing by hand — the same "automation stops one hand-finishing step short of done" gap this log already treats as real lost time elsewhere (Rebar Cage Copier, Section Batch Generator). No market add-in targets this specifically — it is this repo's own pipeline's own gap — but the manual workaround it currently forces is exactly the repetitive clicking this toolkit otherwise exists to remove.

**Description:**
`Stair Landing Completer.pushbutton`, a standalone repair tool runnable any time after `stairs.sync` — it reads stairs already placed in the model, not the sheet, so it also catches dog-legs built in earlier sessions before this tool existed:

1. **Discover pairs** — collect every placed `Stairs` via `structural/stairs.placed(doc)` (`structural/stairs.py:291-306`), but re-keyed by **mark** instead of the first-run-midpoint key that function documents as being for the sheet planner's own matching purposes; every mark shared by exactly two `Stairs` elements is a dog-leg candidate.
2. **Check the gap** — for each pair, confirm the two flights are end-to-end adjacent (base/top level and run endpoint check, mirroring the adjacency `parse_stairs()` already establishes upstream on sheet rows, applied here to built geometry instead) and that no landing already sits between them; pairs that already have one are reported clean and skipped, not silently dropped.
3. **Preview list** — one row per candidate pair: mark, both element ids, base/top levels, a jump-to-element action zooming both flights into view; every row pre-ticked, since "adjacent same-mark pair, no landing" is unambiguous once confirmed.
4. **Place the landing** — re-enter each pair through a fresh `StairsEditScope`, using the same `_swallow_warnings()` commit pattern `structural/stairs.py:257,261` already runs, and call `StairsLanding.CreateAutomaticLanding(doc, first_run, second_run)` — the exact call proven at `cad2bim/builders/stairs.py:303` — once both runs are addressable from one scope; where that isn't achievable for two independently-created `Stairs` elements, fall back to `StairsLanding.CreateSketchedLanding(doc, stairs_id, loop, relative_elevation)` (proven at `cad2bim/builders/stairs.py:388`) sketched across the facing edges, and report which path each pair used.
5. Runs through `commands/_placement.outside_a_transaction` (`commands/_placement.py:174`) exactly as `stairs.sync` itself must, since `StairsEditScope` refuses to start inside an open transaction.
6. Result report: placed / already-had-a-landing / blocked-with-reason (non-adjacent flights, mismatched levels, or a merge-not-supported fallback), same finding shape `commands/stairs.py` already emits elsewhere.

**Implementation:**
- New `anongee_toolkit.structural.stair_landings` module: `pairs_by_mark(doc)` (re-keys `structural/stairs.placed()`'s output), `landing_gap(pair)` (adjacency + existing-landing check), `place_landing(doc, pair)` (wraps the `CreateAutomaticLanding` call with the `CreateSketchedLanding` fallback).
- Reuses `structural/stairs.placed()` (`structural/stairs.py:291`) for discovery, `_swallow_warnings()` (`structural/stairs.py:261`) for the commit, and `commands/_placement.outside_a_transaction` (`commands/_placement.py:174`) for the transaction-free run shape — all three already proven by `stairs.sync` itself.
- `StairsLanding.CreateAutomaticLanding`/`CreateSketchedLanding` call signatures copied verbatim from their one working call site (`cad2bim/builders/stairs.py:303,388`) rather than re-derived from API docs, since that is the only place in the repo they have ever run against a real model.
- New pushbutton bundle (`bundle.yaml`/`script.py`/`README.md`) alongside `Convert Slab.pushbutton`/`Draw Floor.pushbutton` in `Essential.panel/StrucBIM.stack/`, using the same modeless checklist preview shell every other batch repair tool in the repo already runs on.

**Future update:**
- Wire the same pair/gap check into `stairs.sync`'s own `_apply()` (`commands/stairs.py:65`) as a post-placement flag, so a dog-leg's missing landing surfaces the moment it's placed instead of waiting for a separate audit pass — the same "audit becomes a built-in preflight" progression the already-logged Naming Convention Auditor proposes for RC Automation's own duplicate-type risk.
- If merging two independently-created `Stairs` elements into one scope proves reliable, retrofit `commands/stairs.py`'s `_apply()` to build both flights of a dog-leg inside one shared `StairsEditScope` from the start, so future `stairs.sync` runs place the landing inline and this tool becomes a one-time repair pass for stairs built before the fix, not a permanent step in the workflow.
- Extend pairing past two-row marks to three-or-more-flight scissor/return stairs once the dog-leg case is proven, reading whatever grouping shape `rc_automation/placement.py`'s `parse_stairs()` gives a longer run.
- Push the same boundary-resolution engine the other direction: a "Finish Type Consistency" check that verifies a wall's actual built type matches what every room touching it already reports, catching a wall swapped to the wrong type after room finishes were already signed off.

## 2026-09-22 10:39 UTC

**Idea name:** Duplicate Instance Finder & Merger — flags coincident-location elements of the same category and type sitting exactly on top of each other (a copy-paste slip, a "Paste Aligned → Same Place" repeated by habit, a linked model accidentally bound into the host), the one everyday model-hygiene pass Revit's own warning log does not run and this repo's own dead bounding-box helper has never been pointed at

**Potential:**
Copying a column, wall, beam or generic-model instance and pasting it back "Aligned to Same Place" — the exact workflow a modeler reaches for after moving a family into position by trial and error, or reflexively hits twice out of habit — produces two fully valid, fully overlapping elements, and Revit raises **no warning for it**: unlike a genuinely illegal condition (two walls sketched on the same line in the same sketch, which Revit does flag), two independently-placed family instances occupying identical space are each individually legitimate, so the warning dialog stays silent while the schedule, the quantity take-off and the rebar/BBS count silently double for that one element. This is a well-known, recurring failure mode on any project that goes through more than one round of copy-paste coordination or a link-to-host bind gone wrong, and it compounds exactly where this toolkit already does its heaviest lifting: a duplicated column or footing does not just inflate a schedule row, it gives `BBS Generator.pushbutton` a second full rebar cage to count and `structural/inventory.py`'s own category counts (`_categories()`, `inventory.py:73`) a silently wrong total the moment anyone reads the model's own self-description. The repo already has exactly the primitive this needs, built once and used for something else entirely: `anongee_toolkit.revit.geometry.bounding_boxes_overlap()` (`revit/geometry.py:40`) is public API — re-exported in `anongee_toolkit/__init__.py:108,223` and `anongee_toolkit/revit/__init__.py:13,37` — and `grep -rn "bounding_boxes_overlap" AnonGee.extension` finds exactly one real caller outside its own definition: `BIMGeneration.pushbutton/core.py:369-373` doesn't even import the shared one, it hand-rolls its own near-identical copy, `_bounding_boxes_overlap()`, and uses it only to decide whether to `JoinGeometryUtils.JoinGeometry()` a column against a beam (`core.py:489-490`) — a completely different question (do these two touch?) from the one this idea asks (are these two the *same* element, twice?), and answered with a *loose* tolerance (`tolerance=-0.05`, i.e. boxes must overlap by more than half an inch before joining) rather than the tight, near-zero tolerance a true duplicate check needs. So today the one exported helper capable of this check has a hand-rolled duplicate of itself sitting right next to it, and neither copy has ever been asked "is this a duplicate instance," only "should I join these." On the market, this exact check — coincident-instance detection across categories — exists only as one line item inside paid whole-model QA/health-check suites (Ideate Sidekick's "Model Health" duplicate scan, CTC BIM Tools' Health Check, BIM One's eTools Pro "Duplicate Finder"), each licensed per seat as part of a much larger bundle; Revit's own Purge Unused and warning log, by design, catch neither case, because a duplicate instance is neither "unused" (both copies are placed and visible) nor "invalid" (each one individually is a perfectly legal element).

**Description:**
`Duplicate Instance Finder.pushbutton`, a new sibling inside the existing `Bulk Tool.stack` (alongside `Bulk Delete.pulldown` / `Bulk Rename.pulldown` / `Rebar.pulldown`), since this is a whole-model hygiene sweep in the same family as the bulk-delete tools already living there:

1. **Scope + category picker** — whole model or active view; category list seeded from the same category walk `structural/inventory.py:73`'s `_categories()` already runs, defaulting to the categories this toolkit places (columns, framing, footings, floors/foundation slabs, walls, generic models) but not restricted to them — any placed category can be checked.
2. **Group by Category + TypeId** — only two instances of the *same* Type can be a true duplicate; two overlapping instances of different types is a real design decision (a haunch beam sharing space with a drop panel, say), not a mistake, so different-type overlaps are never flagged. Grouping mirrors the same category/type walk `structural/inventory.py:117`'s `_families()` already does over every `ElementType`, adapted here to instances.
3. **Signature match within each group**, picked by how the family is hosted:
   - Point-hosted (columns, generic models): compare `instance.Location.Point` pairwise at a tight default tolerance (2 mm) plus matching rotation and host level — the same property read already proven at `structural/columns.py:297`.
   - Curve-hosted (beams, framing, walls): compare `instance.Location.Curve` endpoint pairs (either order) at the same tolerance — the same read already proven at `structural/framing.py:258`.
   - Sketch-hosted (floors, foundation slabs): compare the boundary loop's vertex set after sort/round, extracted via the floor's `Sketch` element the exact way `ConvertFloor.pushbutton/script.py:390,397` already pulls one out (`el.GetDependentElements(DB.ElementClassFilter(DB.Sketch))`).
   - Anything else: fall back to `bounding_boxes_overlap()` itself (`revit/geometry.py:40`), called at a tight duplicate-detection tolerance rather than the loose join-check tolerance `BIMGeneration.pushbutton` uses it at today.
4. **Preview checklist**, one cluster per coincident group (2+ instances): the lowest-ElementId instance in each cluster is marked "keep" and shown unticked, every other instance in the cluster is marked "duplicate" and pre-ticked for deletion — same pre-ticked-safe-default convention `Bulk Purge`'s zero-usage rows and `Type Consolidator`'s drifted clusters both already use. Each duplicate row also shows a dependency count (hosted tags, dimensions, rebar) so a duplicate that actually carries something real hosted on it doesn't vanish as a surprise.
5. **Delete** — one `Transaction`, ticked duplicates deleted; result report ("Removed N duplicate instances across M clusters, K left for review") exportable to Excel via the same `openpyxl` write-back path `Export Schedule.pushbutton` / `BBS Generator.pushbutton` already use, giving the run a before/after audit trail.

**Implementation:**
- New `anongee_toolkit/operations/duplicate_finder.py`, parallel to the existing `operations/bulk_delete.py`: `group_by_category_type(doc, categories)` (adapts the walk `structural/inventory.py:117`'s `_families()` already runs), `find_clusters(group, tol_mm=2.0)` dispatching to point/curve/sketch/bbox comparators, and `merge(doc, clusters, keep="lowest_id")`.
- `bounding_boxes_overlap()` (`revit/geometry.py:40`) is imported directly from `anongee_toolkit.revit`, becoming this function's first real cross-category caller — `BIMGeneration.pushbutton/core.py:369`'s hand-rolled `_bounding_boxes_overlap()` is left untouched (it solves a different, still-valid problem: join tolerance, not duplicate tolerance), but this closes the gap of the shared helper never being used for the thing its own docstring ("bounding-box overlap tests") already promises.
- Point/curve location reads copy `structural/columns.py:297` (`instance.Location.Point`) and `structural/framing.py:258` (`instance.Location.Curve`) verbatim rather than re-deriving the `Location` cast pattern.
- Floor/slab sketch-boundary extraction reuses `ConvertFloor.pushbutton/script.py:390,397`'s `GetDependentElements(DB.ElementClassFilter(DB.Sketch))` call, the only place in the repo that has ever pulled a `Sketch` element back out of a placed floor.
- Dependency count per duplicate uses plain `GetDependentElements(None)`, the same unfiltered call already proven at `ConvertFloor.pushbutton/script.py:581`.
- Preview grid reuses `anongee_toolkit/ui/checklist.py`'s `CheckList`, the same CPython3-safe shell every other batch tool in this repo already builds its dialog on.
- New pushbutton bundle (`bundle.yaml`/`script.py`/`README.md`) added directly inside `AnonGee.tab/Essential.panel/Bulk Tool.stack/`, no new panel or stack needed.

**Future update:**
- A lower-confidence "possible duplicate" tier for near-but-not-exact matches (offset by a few centimetres rather than coincident) — flagged for review but unticked by default, catching a duplicate that was nudged slightly after being pasted.
- A cross-link mode: compare host-model instances against a selected linked model's transformed geometry, to catch the "linked model accidentally bound into the host" case named above, which today's same-document-only scope can't see.
- Feed confirmed clusters into the already-logged Warning Auditor as one more finding category once both tools exist, so a duplicate-instance sweep becomes part of the same batch-resolve pass as native Revit warnings instead of a separate button to remember.

## 2026-09-22 15:42 UTC

**Idea name:** Base Point & Survey Datum Auditor — an interactive, in-session pushbutton front end for the toolkit's own Project-Base-Point/Survey-Point offset engine, so a modeler can see and safely correct a model's dual-elevation datum without ever opening the Excel bridge

**Potential:**
This repo already has a small, carefully-written module that answers a question every structural team eventually gets wrong — "what does this level read on the survey/site drawing versus what Revit itself calls its elevation, and are those two numbers even talking about the same building?" `anongee_toolkit/structural/coordinates.py` (133 lines total) exists purely for this: its own module docstring states the problem outright — "So a level at 3.3 on a structural drawing and the same level at 103.3 on a site drawing are the same level, and the difference between them is a property of the model rather than of the sheet" (`coordinates.py:9-11`). `survey_offset_mm(doc)` (`coordinates.py:22-57`) reads `BasePoint.GetProjectBasePoint(doc)`/`BasePoint.GetSurveyPoint(doc)` and returns the live offset in millimetres, or `None` with a stated reason when it can't be read; `set_survey_offset_mm(doc, wanted_mm)` (`coordinates.py:60-124`) is the one function in the module that writes, and it does so carefully — unpinning the (pinned-by-default) survey point, writing `BASEPOINT_ELEVATION_PARAM`, then re-pinning it exactly as found (`coordinates.py:92-121`) — with the docstring stating why it is off by default: "moving a survey point re-coordinates a whole project… That is a decision somebody makes on purpose" (`coordinates.py:14-16`, repeated at `62-68`). But grepping the whole toolkit for `BasePoint`/`SurveyPoint` turns up exactly one real source file besides `coordinates.py` itself: its one and only caller, `anongee_toolkit/commands/levels.py` (`levels.py:162,170,175,187`) — the headless, bridge-only implementation of `levels.sync`. That command reads `SURVEY_OFFSET`/`APPLY_SURVEY_OFFSET` off an `INFO` sheet cell, and the actual dual-elevation reconciliation it feeds lives in `rc_automation/datum.py`'s `resolve_elevations()`, which converts a survey-measured row via `row.elevation_mm = row.elevation_survey_mm - survey_offset_mm` and flags a row that states both elevations and disagrees with the model's own offset via `expected = row.elevation_mm + survey_offset_mm` checked against `ELEVATION_TOLERANCE_MM`. This is not a theoretical gap: `todo-list.md`'s **BUG-25** (`testing`, line 779) is this exact mismatch happening in a real run — *"Reading the `FINDINGS` sheet from a real run: seven warnings, one per level, all saying the survey elevation disagreed. The model puts its survey point at the origin; the workbook says 100 m. One wrong cell, reported seven times."* — a discrepancy that only ever surfaced as a warning buried seven times over in an Excel `FINDINGS` sheet, and per `BUG-32` (line 335) that same sheet gets wiped by the next command run anyway. A modeler working directly in Revit — this toolkit's daily audience, per every one of the 45 already-logged pushbutton ideas — has no way to ask "is our base point/survey point relationship even sane" without that entire Excel/HTTP round trip; `AutoLevel.pushbutton` (the repo's one interactive level tool) reads and writes level elevations through its own planner, entirely in Project-Base terms, and never touches `BasePoint` at all. The already-logged **Link Position Guard** idea explicitly scoped this module out of its own remit, in its own words: *"`anongee_toolkit.structural.coordinates` looks adjacent but solves a different axis entirely… it says nothing about a link's XY position or rotation, which is a separate number tracking a separate problem"* — confirming this gap was seen and deliberately left for a later idea, not missed. On the market, native Revit offers no batch view of this at all: the Project Base Point and Survey Point are two separate, one-at-a-time Properties dialogs, and a level's Elevation Base (Project/Survey) toggle is set per level with no table anywhere comparing what each level reads under both. Coordinate-reconciliation tooling otherwise appears only as a minor feature bundled inside paid site/civil-coordination suites; nothing free gives a structural modeler a single table of every level's dual elevation, backed by the model's own live offset, with a guarded one-click way to correct it.

**Description:**
`Base Point Auditor.pushbutton`, new sibling to `RoundElementDist.pushbutton` in `Essential.panel/StrucBIM.stack` — the stack that already owns "read live datum/structural state, report first, approve each fix" tools, the posture `RoundElementDist.pushbutton`'s own tooltip states:

1. **Read** — on open, calls `coordinates.survey_offset_mm(doc)` for the model's live offset and note, and `structural.levels.levels(doc)` for every level (name, `ElementId`, Project elevation in mm) — the first interactive caller either function has ever had.
2. **Dual-elevation table** — one row per level: Project elevation (native), derived Survey elevation (`project_elevation_mm + offset_mm`, the same formula `datum.py` already uses to check agreement, run here in the open direction instead of buried in a headless diff), and the level's own Elevation Base toggle read off its type parameter, so a mismatch between what a level claims to be measured from and what the table shows is visible at a glance.
3. **State panel** — the Project Base Point's and Survey Point's raw elevation, pinned state, and whether either read as unreachable (`coordinates.py:42-43`'s "this model has no project base point or survey point" case reported plainly rather than silently skipped).
4. **Optional correction** — a modeler types one authoritative number (from a survey/site drawing) against the offset; the tool previews what every level's derived Survey elevation would become, and only on explicit confirm calls `coordinates.set_survey_offset_mm(doc, wanted_mm)` inside a named `Transaction`, reusing its existing unpin/set/re-pin sequence verbatim rather than reimplementing the pinned-element trap it already solved.
5. **Report only by default** — matching `set_survey_offset_mm`'s own documented restraint, the write step is opt-in and requires the same explicit confirmation `levels.sync`'s own `APPLY_SURVEY_OFFSET` switch already demands — here as a checklist tick rather than an Excel cell.
6. **Export** — the same `openpyxl` path every other batch tool in the repo already writes its report through, so a coordination-meeting record of "our survey offset was 0mm, corrected to 100000mm on 2026-09-xx" leaves a documented trail rather than living only in an Excel `FINDINGS` row that `BUG-32` will overwrite on the next bridge run.

**Implementation:**
- No new structural module needed for the core read/write path — this pushbutton's whole job is to be the first interactive caller of `anongee_toolkit.structural.coordinates.survey_offset_mm`/`set_survey_offset_mm` and `anongee_toolkit.structural.levels.levels`, all already written and currently reachable only through the bridge.
- New thin module `anongee_toolkit.structural.datum_report` for exactly one new piece of logic: `dual_elevation_rows(doc)` — zips `levels.levels(doc)` with `coordinates.survey_offset_mm(doc)` into display rows, plus a per-level read of the Elevation Base `BuiltInParameter` — since nothing today assembles Project+Survey elevation into one row for display rather than for a plan/diff pipeline.
- Correction step wraps `coordinates.set_survey_offset_mm` in a single `Transaction`, mirroring the transaction shape `commands/levels.py`'s survey-move handling already uses for the identical call, just interactive instead of envelope-driven.
- Same modeless window shell (`anongee_toolkit.ui.hostwnd`/`checklist`/`theme`) and `openpyxl` export path every other batch/audit tool in the repo already runs on; own `bundle.yaml`/`README.md`, tooltip modeled on `RoundElementDist.pushbutton`'s own "report first, approve each fix" phrasing since both tools share that exact posture.
- Sits in `Essential.panel/StrucBIM.stack`, next to `RoundElementDist.pushbutton`, `DrawFloor.pushbutton`, `ConvertFloor.pushbutton` — the stack that already owns single-document, live-datum, report-then-commit tools.

**Future update:**
- Extend the same table to every `RevitLinkInstance`'s Position mode (Auto-Origin/Auto-Center/By Shared Coordinates), so a modeler can see in one screen whether the model's own datum and every consultant link's positioning mode actually agree — a natural pairing with the already-logged Link Position Guard's transform-drift baseline, but answering "is coordination even configured correctly" rather than "did something move since we last checked."
- Wire the read-only half as its own bridge command (`datum.audit`) so a pre-flight check can run headless before `levels.sync`, catching a BUG-25-shaped disagreement before a workbook is even posted.
- A saved "expected survey elevation per level" list (from a civil/site drawing), so the table becomes pass/fail per level instead of a single flat model-wide offset, catching a level whose own type mistakenly overrides Elevation Base independently of the rest.
- Feed a confirmed offset correction into the already-logged Sheet Issue Kit's since-last-run diff, so a datum change shows up as "what changed since the last issue" alongside sheet and standards changes.
